First works out\n  what the code actually does, then searches THIS skill library for the\n  specialised skills that match the code's security-relevant behaviour, then\n  applies those skills to audit the code and reports findings with CWE ids and\n  fixes. Use this whenever asked to review, audit, or security-check source\n  code, do a secure code review, find vulnerabilities, or check code against the\n  OWASP Top 10 or CWE. Keywords: code review, secure code review, source code\n  audit, find vulnerabilities, security assessment of code, OWASP, CWE, SQL\n  injection, weak cryptography, hardcoded secrets, insecure deserialization,\n  XSS, SSRF, authentication and session flaws, JWT verification, password\n  storage.\ndomain: cybersecurity\nsubdomain: application-security\ntags:\n  - code-review\n  - security-review\n  - owasp\n  - cwe\n  - sast\n  - dispatcher\nversion: \"1.1\"\nlicense: Apache-2.0\n---\n# Reviewing Code for Security Vulnerabilities\n\n## Overview\n\nThis is a router skill. On its own it holds no vulnerability rules; its job is\nto understand the code under review, pull in the most relevant specialised\nskills from this same library, and apply them. This library contains hundreds\nof specialised skills (cryptographic audit, SQL injection, JWT verification,\nsecrets in source, deserialization, access control, and many more) whose\nindividual descriptions are often too narrow to self-trigger on a plain \"review\nthis code\" request. This skill is the entry point that finds and uses them.\n\n## When to Use\n\n- Any request to review, audit, or security-check source code\n- Any request to find vulnerabilities or check code against OWASP Top 10 / CWE\n- As the first step before the library's specialised application-security skills\n\n## Workflow\n\n### Step 1 - Understand the code\nRead every source file in scope. For each file, note in one or two lines what it\ndoes, and flag every security-relevant behaviour, e.g.:\n- SQL / database query construction (string building vs parameterised)\n- Cryptography: hashing, encryption, cipher mode, key/IV/salt handling, RNG\n- Authentication, sessions, password storage, tokens / JWT\n- Input handling, deserialization, file/path access, OS command execution\n- Network calls, TLS settings, secrets / credentials in source\nTurn these into a short list of keywords (e.g. \"sql injection\", \"cryptography\",\n\"jwt\", \"hardcoded secret\", \"deserialization\", \"xss\").\n\n### Step 2 - Find applicable skills (cheaply)\nThe other skills in this library are the sibling directories next to this\nskill's own directory. Do NOT read them all. Instead:\n1. Find this skill's directory, then its parent (the library root).\n2. Grep skill NAMES first for your Step 1 keywords, e.g. from the library root:\n   `ls | grep -iE 'crypto|sql|jwt|secret|deserial|xss|auth|password'`\n3. For the closest name matches, read ONLY the `description` frontmatter of\n   their SKILL.md (`sed -n '1,15p' <name>/SKILL.md`).\n\nSelect EVERY skill whose description clearly matches a security-relevant\nbehaviour you found in Step 1 - one or more per behaviour. Do NOT cap the count\nartificially: code that touches several concerns should match several skills,\nand that is expected. Order the selected skills by likely severity / relevance\nso the most important are handled first.\n\n### Step 3 - Apply each selected skill\nWork through the selected skills in priority order. Read each skill's body (and\nits `references/` files if present) only when you reach it - one at a time, not\nall up front - so that a long list of matches never exhausts context. Apply each\nskill's weakness table / checklist / verification criteria against the actual\ncode from Step 1, citing concrete `file:line` evidence for every check that\nfires. Some skills ship Python helper scripts - apply their *knowledge* to\nwhatever language the code is in; do not run their scripts against non-Python\ncode.\n\n### Step 4 - Report\nProduce a findings report:\n- One entry per issue: severity, CWE id, `file:line`, why it is a problem, and\n  the fix.\n- A \"Skills applied\" list naming the specialised skills you used.\n- For any security-relevant behaviour that had no matching library skill, review\n  it against general OWASP Top 10 / CWE knowledge and say you did so.\n- If nothing is wrong, say so explicitly and list what you checked.\n\n## Constraints\n\n- Report only. Do not create or modify any files unless explicitly asked.\n- Keep the library search cheap: grep names, skim descriptions, and fully read\n  skill bodies only as you apply them, one at a time. Never load the whole\n  library into context at once.\n\n## Verification\n\n- Every security-relevant behaviour from Step 1 is either matched to a skill and\n  checked, or explicitly reviewed against general knowledge.\n- Every finding carries a CWE id and a `file:line`.\n- The \"Skills applied\" list is present.\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

Because {@link Db} confines the\n    14\t * connection to one caller and {@link Throttle} takes row locks, \"is this source still allowed\n    15\t * to register, then create the row\" is a single transaction: a rollback restores both the row\n    16\t * and the budget, and no interleaving can let two registrations both see the mailbox as free.\n    17\t *\n    18\t * <p>Duplicates are answered with a fixed message that is also what a successful registration\n    19\t * prints, so this endpoint does not enumerate accounts. That is a deliberate trade-off against\n    20\t * the \"identical failure message\" rule and is discussed in the README: registration must accept\n    21\t * that a mailbox may already be taken, and the only disclosure is that a mailbox exists, which\n    22\t * an attacker learns by trying to sign in anyway. Sign-in, where the rule really matters, is\n    23\t * uniform.\n    24\t */\n    25\tpublic final class AccountStore implements AutoCloseable {\n    26\t\n    27\t    /** Opaque row handle: the primary key plus the stored credential, never a password. */\n    28\t    public record Account(long id, String email, String username, String passwordHash,\n    29\t        int kdfMemoryKib, int kdfIterations, int kdfParallelism, long passwordUpdatedAt) {\n    30\t    }\n    31\t\n    32\t    public enum RegisterStatus {\n    33\t        /** The account now exists and was created by this call. */\n    34\t        CREATED,\n    35\t        /** The mailbox or username was already taken; the caller must not report which. */\n    36\t        DUPLICATE,\n    37\t        /** The source's registration budget is spent. */\n    38\t        THROTTLED\n    39\t    }\n    40\t\n    41\t    public record RegisterOutcome(RegisterStatus status, String message) {\n    42\t    }\n    43\t\n    44\t    /**\n    45\t     * Shared wording for both outcomes of a registration attempt, so a refusal cannot confirm\n    46\t     * or deny the existence of an account.\n    47\t     */\n    48\t    public static final String OUTCOME_MESSAGE =\n    49\t        \"If the details are valid and the account is available, registration is complete.\";\n    50\t\n    51\t    private final Db db;\n    52\t    private final PasswordHasher hasher;\n    53\t    private final Throttle throttle;\n    54\t    private final Gate gate;\n    55\t    private final Clock clock;\n    56\t\n    57\t    public AccountStore(Db db, PasswordHasher hasher, Throttle throttle, Gate gate, Clock clock) {\n    58\t        this.db = db;\n    59\t        this.hasher = hasher;\n    60\t        this.throttle = throttle;\n    61\t        this.gate = gate;\n    62\t        this.clock = clock;\n    63\t    }\n    64\t\n    65\t    /**\n    66\t     * Creates an account. The caller passes the already-validated canonical email and username;\n    67\t     * validation happens first so the refusal reason can be reported before any key-derivation\n    68\t     * cost is paid, and so a rejected registration never reaches the store.\n    69\t     *\n    70\t     * <p>A duplicate raises {@link DuplicateAccount} out of the transaction, which rolls the\n    71\t     * whole thing back - including the source-budget consume - so being refused for someone\n    72\t     * else having taken the mailbox cannot burn the caller's quota.\n    73\t     *\n    74\t     * <p>Every outcome is recorded <em>inside</em> the transaction that produces it, so an\n    75\t     * account cannot exist with no trail behind it: if the acceptance record cannot be written,\n    76\t     * the insert is rolled back and the caller is told the service is unavailable. Recording here\n    77\t     * rather than in the command line also means an embedded caller cannot skip the trail.\n    78\t     */\n    79\t    public RegisterOutcome register(char[] password, String canonicalEmail, String canonicalUsername,\n    80\t        String sourceId) throws SQLException, AuditLog.AuditException {\n    81\t        boolean created;\n    82\t        try {\n    83\t            created = db.inTransaction(connection -> {\n    84\t                gate.record(AuditLog.Event.REGISTRATION_ATTEMPTED,\n    85\t                    \"email\", gate.emailTag(canonicalEmail), \"username\", gate.usernameTag(canonicalUsername),\n    86\t                    \"source\", gate.sourceTag(sourceId));\n    87\t                if (!throttle.tryConsumeSource(connection, Throttle.REGISTER_SOURCE_SCOPE, sourceId)) {\n    88\t                    gate.record(AuditLog.Event.REGISTRATION_THROTTLED,\n    89\t                        \"source\", gate.sourceTag(sourceId));\n    90\t                    return Boolean.FALSE;\n    91\t                }\n    92\t                // The stored cost columns are read back out of the hash string rather than taken\n    93\t                // from configuration, so they cannot drift from the credential they describe.\n    94\t                String hash = hasher.encode(password);\n    95\t                PasswordHasher.Cost cost = hasher.costOf(hash);\n    96\t                long now = clock.instant().getEpochSecond();\n    97\t                try (PreparedStatement insert = connection.prepareStatement(\"\"\"\n    98\t                    INSERT INTO accounts (email, username, password_hash, kdf, kdf_memory_kib,\n    99\t                                          kdf_iterations, kdf_parallelism, created_at, password_updated_at)\n   100\t                    VALUES (?, ?, ?, 'argon2id', ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)\n   101\t                    \"\"\")) {\n   102\t                    insert.setString(1, canonicalEmail);\n   103\t                    insert.setString(2, canonicalUsername);\n   104\t                    insert.setString(3, hash);\n   105\t                    insert.setInt(4, cost.memoryKib());\n   106\t                    insert.setInt(5, cost.iterations());\n   107\t                    insert.setInt(6, cost.parallelism());\n   108\t                    insert.setLong(7, now);\n   109\t                    insert.setLong(8, now);\n   110\t                    insert.executeUpdate();\n   111\t                } catch (SQLException rejected) {\n   112\t                    if (Throttle.isUniqueViolation(rejected)) {\n   113\t                        throw new DuplicateAccount(rejected);\n   114\t                    }\n   115\t                    throw rejected;\n   116\t                }\n   117\t                // Written while the transaction is still open: if this record cannot be persisted\n   118\t                // the insert rolls back with it, so an account is never left with no trail.\n   119\t                gate.record(AuditLog.Event.REGISTRATION_ACCEPTED,\n   120\t                    \"email\", gate.emailTag(canonicalEmail), \"username\", gate.usernameTag(canonicalUsername),\n   121\t                    \"source\", gate.sourceTag(sourceId));\n   122\t                return Boolean.TRUE;\n   123\t            });\n   124\t        } catch (DuplicateAccount alreadyTaken) {\n   125\t            // The insert rolled back, but a refusal is exactly the event that must be on record.\n   126\t            gate.record(AuditLog.Event.REGISTRATION_DUPLICATE,\n   127\t                \"email\", gate.emailTag(canonicalEmail), \"username\", gate.usernameTag(canonicalUsername),\n   128\t                \"source\", gate.sourceTag(sourceId));\n   129\t            // The rollback above also undid the source-budget consume, so charge it again here. Without\n   130\t            // this, submitting an always-taken mailbox forces a full key-derivation and a store write\n   131\t            // for free, every time - the per-source limit would bound only the cheap path. Charging\n   132\t            // even on a duplicate is what makes the limit bound the expensive path; the cost to a\n   133\t            // legitimate caller whose mailbox happened to be taken is one token of a generous window.\n   134\t            chargeDuplicateAttempt(sourceId);\n   135\t            return new RegisterOutcome(RegisterStatus.DUPLICATE, OUTCOME_MESSAGE);\n   136\t        }\n   137\t        return new RegisterOutcome(created ? RegisterStatus.CREATED : RegisterStatus.THROTTLED, OUTCOME_MESSAGE);\n   138\t    }\n   139\t\n   140\t    /**\n   141\t     * Charges one source-budget token for a duplicate registration attempt, in its own committed\n   142\t     * transaction. The register transaction's rollback already undid the consume made before the\n   143\t     * insert, so without this a caller could force the server's most expensive work (a full Argon2id\n   144\t     * derivation plus a write) an unbounded number of times by always submitting a taken mailbox,\n   145\t     * without ever spending the per-source budget that is meant to bound it. Committing separately is\n   146\t     * what lets the charge survive the rollback of the rejected insert.\n   147\t     */\n   148\t    private void chargeDuplicateAttempt(String sourceId) throws SQLException, AuditLog.AuditException {\n   149\t        db.inTransaction(connection -> {\n   150\t            throttle.tryConsumeSource(connection, Throttle.REGISTER_SOURCE_SCOPE, sourceId);\n   151\t            return Boolean.TRUE;\n   152\t        });\n   153\t    }\n   154\t\n   155\t    /** Signals a taken mailbox/username without carrying any of the conflicting value. */\n   156\t    static final class DuplicateAccount extends RuntimeException {\n   157\t        private static final long serialVersionUID = 1L;\n   158\t\n   159\t        DuplicateAccount(SQLException cause) {\n   160\t            super(\"duplicate\", cause);\n   161\t        }\n   162\t    }\n   163\t\n   164\t    public Account findByEmail(String canonicalEmail) throws SQLException, AuditLog.AuditException {\n   165\t        return select(\"email = ?\", canonicalEmail);\n   166\t    }\n   167\t\n   168\t    public Account findByUsername(String canonicalUsername) throws SQLException, AuditLog.AuditException {\n   169\t        return select(\"username = ?\", canonicalUsername);\n   170\t    }\n   171\t\n   172\t    public Account findById(long id) throws SQLException, AuditLog.AuditException {\n   173\t        return select(\"id = ?\", Long.valueOf(id));\n   174\t    }\n   175\t\n   176\t    /** Binds by type, so a credential-shaped string can never influence which column is matched. */\n   177\t    private Account select(String predicate, Object value) throws SQLException, AuditLog.AuditException {\n   178\t        String sql = \"SELECT id, email, username, password_hash, kdf_memory_kib, kdf_iterations,\"\n   179\t            + \" kdf_parallelism, password_updated_at FROM accounts WHERE \" + predicate;\n   180\t        return db.inTransaction(connection -> {\n   181\t            try (PreparedStatement query = connection.prepareStatement(sql)) {\n   182\t                if (value instanceof Long asLong) {\n   183\t                    query.setLong(1, asLong);\n   184\t                } else {\n   185\t                    query.setString(1, (String) value);\n   186\t                }\n   187\t                try (ResultSet found = query.executeQuery()) {\n   188\t                    if (!found.next()) {\n   189\t                        return null;\n   190\t                    }\n   191\t                    return new Account(found.getLong(1), found.getString(2), found.getString(3),\n   192\t                        found.getString(4), found.getInt(5), found.getInt(6), found.getInt(7),\n   193\t                        found.getLong(8));\n   194\t                }\n   195\t            }\n   196\t        });\n   197\t    }\n   198\t\n   199\t    /**\n   200\t     * Replaces a credential after a successful sign-in whose stored parameters are weaker than\n   201\t     * the current policy. Guarded by {@code password_updated_at} so a concurrent password change\n   202\t     * wins and this upgrade is skipped instead of clobbering it.\n   203\t     */\n   204\t    public boolean upgradePasswordHash(long accountId, char[] password, long expectedUpdatedAt)\n   205\t        throws SQLException, AuditLog.AuditException {\n   206\t        String hash = hasher.encode(password);\n   207\t        PasswordHasher.Cost cost = hasher.costOf(hash);\n   208\t        long now = clock.instant().getEpochSecond();\n   209\t        return db.inTransaction(connection -> {\n   210\t            try (PreparedStatement update = connection.prepareStatement(\"\"\"\n   211\t                UPDATE accounts\n   212\t                   SET password_hash = ?, kdf = 'argon2id', kdf_memory_kib = ?, kdf_iterations = ?,\n   213\t                       kdf_parallelism = ?, password_updated_at = ?\n   214\t                 WHERE id = ? AND password_updated_at = ?\n   215\t                \"\"\")) {\n   216\t                update.setString(1, hash);\n   217\t                update.setInt(2, cost.memoryKib());\n   218\t                update.setInt(3, cost.iterations());\n   219\t                update.setInt(4, cost.parallelism());\n   220\t                update.setLong(5, now);\n   221\t                update.setLong(6, accountId);\n   222\t                update.setLong(7, expectedUpdatedAt);\n   223\t                return update.executeUpdate() == 1;\n   224\t            }\n   225\t        });\n   226\t    }\n   227\t\n   228\t    public Db db() {\n   229\t        return db;\n   230\t    }\n   231\t\n   232\t    @Override\n   233\t    public void close() {\n   234\t        db.close();\n   235\t    }\n   236\t}\n===== AuditLog.java =====\n     1\tpackage app;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.io.IOException;\n     4\timport java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets;\n     5\timport java.nio.file.Files;\n     6\timport java.nio.file.LinkOption;\n     7\timport java.nio.file.Path;\n     8\timport java.nio.file.StandardOpenOption;\n     9\timport java.nio.file.attribute.BasicFileAttributes;\n    10\timport java.nio.file.attribute.PosixFilePermission;\n    11\timport java.nio.file.attribute.PosixFilePermissions;\n    12\timport java.time.Instant;\n    13\timport java.util.ArrayList;\n    14\timport java.util.EnumSet;\n    15\timport java.util.List;\n    16\timport java.util.Set;\n    17\t\n    18\t/**\n    19\t * Append-only security audit trail.\n    20\t *\n    21\t * <p>Every field written here is chosen by this program. No externally supplied string -\n    22\t * email, username, password, token, file name - is ever passed to {@link #record}, so log\n    23\t * injection and secret leakage through the log are structural properties rather than\n    24\t * conventions someone has to remember. Where a value must be correlated across records it\n    25\t * appears as a SHA-256 tag, which is not a credential.\n    26\t *\n    27\t * <p>Writes fail closed: {@link Gate} turns an {@link AuditException} into a denial, so an\n    28\t * attacker who replaces the file with a directory or widens its permissions cannot make security\n    29\t * events unrecorded. The size budget is the one case that does not deny: see {@link #truncateToFit}\n    30\t * for why retiring old records beats refusing the feature.\n    31\t */\n    32\tpublic final class AuditLog implements AutoCloseable {\n    33\t\n    34\t    /**\n    35\t     * Upper bound on the trail. Startup enforces it, and an append that would exceed it retires\n    36\t     * the oldest records and says so, rather than refusing every later security decision.\n    37\t     */\n    38\t    public static final long MAX_BYTES = 64L * 1024 * 1024;\n    39\t\n    40\t    private static final Set<PosixFilePermission> OWNER_ONLY =\n    41\t        EnumSet.of(PosixFilePermission.OWNER_READ, PosixFilePermission.OWNER_WRITE);\n    42\t\n    43\t    /** Event names are a fixed vocabulary - never derived from input. */\n    44\t    public enum Event {\n    45\t        REGISTRATION_ATTEMPTED,\n    46\t        REGISTRATION_ACCEPTED,\n    47\t        REGISTRATION_DUPLICATE,\n    48\t        REGISTRATION_THROTTLED,\n    49\t        REGISTRATION_REFUSED_INVALID_INPUT,\n    50\t        LOGIN_SUCCESS,\n    51\t        LOGIN_FAILURE,\n    52\t        LOGIN_REFUSED_INVALID_INPUT,\n    53\t        LOGIN_THROTTLED_ACCOUNT,\n    54\t        LOGIN_THROTTLED_SOURCE,\n    55\t        LOGIN_STORE_ERROR,\n    56\t        ACCOUNT_LOCKED,\n    57\t        ACCOUNT_UNLOCKED,\n    58\t        ADMIN_REVOKE_REQUESTED,\n    59\t        ADMIN_REVOKE_REFUSED_INVALID_INPUT,\n    60\t        MAINTENANCE_RUN,\n    61\t        TRAIL_RETURED,\n    62\t        SESSION_ISSUED,\n    63\t        SESSION_REJECTED,\n    64\t        SESSION_REVOKED\n    65\t    }\n    66\t\n    67\t    private final Path file;\n    68\t    private final Object writeLock = new Object();\n    69\t    private long bytes = -1;\n    70\t\n    71\t    public AuditLog(Path file) {\n    72\t        this.file = file;\n    73\t    }\n    74\t\n    75\t    /** Writes one record; throws if it could not be persisted, so callers can fail closed. */\n    76\t    public void record(Event event, String... fields) throws AuditException {\n    77\t        if (event == null) {\n    78\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"event is required\");\n    79\t        }\n    80\t        if (fields.length % 2 != 0) {\n    81\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"fields must be key/value pairs\");\n    82\t        }\n    83\t        StringBuilder line = new StringBuilder(192).append('{');\n    84\t        field(line, \"ts\", Instant.now().toString(), true);\n    85\t        field(line, \"event\", event.name(), false);\n    86\t        for (int i = 0; i < fields.length; i += 2) {\n    87\t            field(line, fields[i], fields[i + 1], false);\n    88\t        }\n    89\t        line.append('}').append('\\n');\n    90\t        byte[] encoded = line.toString().getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8);\n    91\t        synchronized (writeLock) {\n    92\t            try {\n    93\t                guardFile();\n    94\t                if (bytes + encoded.length > MAX_BYTES) {\n    95\t                    truncateToFit(encoded.length);\n    96\t                }\n    97\t                append(encoded);\n    98\t                bytes += encoded.length;\n    99\t            } catch (IOException | AuditException failed) {\n   100\t                throw new AuditException(\"the audit record could not be written\");\n   101\t            }\n   102\t        }\n   103\t    }\n   104\t\n   105\t    /**\n   106\t     * Startup bound: if an existing trail has grown past the budget - which can only happen from\n   107\t     * outside this process - retire it and mark the cut. Called before any command runs.\n   108\t     */\n   109\t    public void retireOversizedTrail() throws AuditException {\n   110\t        synchronized (writeLock) {\n   111\t            try {\n   112\t                guardFile();\n   113\t                if (bytes > MAX_BYTES) {\n   114\t                    restartTrail(bytes);\n   115\t                }\n   116\t            } catch (IOException | AuditException failed) {\n   117\t                throw new AuditException(\"the audit trail could not be retired\");\n   118\t            }\n   119\t        }\n   120\t    }\n   121\t\n   122\t    /**\n   123\t     * Starts a new trail when the budget is full, and says so on the operator's terminal.\n   124\t     *\n   125\t     * <p>Retiring the oldest records is the only behaviour that keeps the feature available. A\n   126\t     * budget that merely refuses would mean that filling the log - which anyone able to trigger\n   127\t     * refusals can do - turns every later sign-in and registration into a denial: a denial of\n   128\t     * service against the whole feature, caused by the audit control itself.\n   129\t     *\n   130\t     * <p>The cut is marked in two channels, not one. The operator sees a note on the terminal, and a\n   131\t     * {@link Event#TRAIL_RETURED} record becomes the new trail's first line carrying the retired byte\n   132\t     * count. The in-trail marker is the load-bearing one: without it, flooding the trail destroys\n   133\t     * every {@code LOGIN_SUCCESS}, {@code ACCOUNT_LOCKED} and {@code ADMIN_REVOKE_REQUESTED} ever\n   134\t     * written and leaves a file indistinguishable from one where nothing happened - the erasure would\n   135\t     * be invisible to anyone who cannot read the terminal. A single record that cannot fit the whole\n   136\t     * budget still fails closed.\n   137\t     */\n   138\t    private void truncateToFit(int incoming) throws IOException, AuditException {\n   139\t        long retired = Math.max(bytes, 0L);\n   140\t        restartTrail(retired);\n   141\t        if (bytes + incoming > MAX_BYTES) {\n   142\t            throw new AuditException(\"a single audit record cannot fit the audit budget\");\n   143\t        }\n   144\t    }\n   145\t\n   146\t    /**\n   147\t     * Deletes the trail, recreates it owner-only, and writes the retirement marker as the new first\n   148\t     * record. Must be called while {@link #writeLock} is held.\n   149\t     */\n   150\t    private void restartTrail(long retiredBytes) throws IOException, AuditException {\n   151\t        Files.delete(file);\n   152\t        bytes = -1;\n   153\t        guardFile();\n   154\t        System.err.println(\"NOTE the audit log reached its \" + MAX_BYTES\n   155\t            + \" byte budget and was restarted; \" + retiredBytes\n   156\t            + \" bytes of older trail were retired\");\n   157\t        StringBuilder line = new StringBuilder(160).append('{');\n   158\t        field(line, \"ts\", Instant.now().toString(), true);\n   159\t        field(line, \"event\", Event.TRAIL_RETURED.name(), false);\n   160\t        field(line, \"retired_bytes\", Long.toString(retiredBytes), false);\n   161\t        field(line, \"budget_bytes\", Long.toString(MAX_BYTES), false);\n   162\t        line.append('}').append('\\n');\n   163\t        byte[] marker = line.toString().getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8);\n   164\t        append(marker);\n   165\t        bytes += marker.length;\n   166\t    }\n   167\t\n   168\t    /**\n   169\t     * Appends to the trail without ever following a symbolic link and without ever creating one.\n   170\t     *\n   171\t     * <p>{@link LinkOption#NOFOLLOW_LINKS} is passed to the open itself, so the kernel - and not a\n   172\t     * check that completed a few instructions earlier - is what refuses a trail that was swapped for\n   173\t     * a link or a directory after {@link #guardFile()} looked at it. Without it, {@code guardFile}\n   174\t     * proves the path is a regular owner-only file and the append then writes wherever the path\n   175\t     * points at the moment of the open, which is a window anyone able to write in the data\n   176\t     * directory can reopen at will.\n   177\t     *\n   178\t     * <p>{@code CREATE} is deliberately absent: a trail that disappears mid-record must make this\n   179\t     * record fail closed - the caller denies - rather than have the append quietly re-create it with\n   180\t     * the process umask, which is typically group- and world-readable. The next record's\n   181\t     * {@link #guardFile()} re-creates the file owner-only, so one record is lost, not the trail.\n   182\t     */\n   183\t    private void append(byte[] encoded) throws IOException {\n   184\t        Files.write(file, encoded, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS,\n   185\t            StandardOpenOption.WRITE, StandardOpenOption.APPEND);\n   186\t    }\n   187\t\n   188\t    private void guardFile() throws IOException, AuditException {\n   189\t        if (Files.isSymbolicLink(file)) {\n   190\t            throw new AuditException(\"the audit log is a symbolic link\");\n   191\t        }\n   192\t        if (!Files.exists(file, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS)) {\n   193\t            // The mode is passed to the create so a recreated trail cannot exist for even an\n   194\t            // instant with the umask default; there is no chmod afterwards, because chmod follows\n   195\t            // links and a create attribute does not.\n   196\t            Files.createFile(file, PosixFilePermissions.asFileAttribute(OWNER_ONLY));\n   197\t        }\n   198\t        if (!Files.isRegularFile(file, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS)) {\n   199\t            throw new AuditException(\"the audit log is not a regular file\");\n   200\t        }\n   201\t        if (!OWNER_ONLY.equals(Files.getPosixFilePermissions(file, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS))) {\n   202\t            throw new AuditException(\"the audit log is not owner-only\");\n   203\t        }\n   204\t        // Re-read rather than trust the running total: the budget bounds the file on disk, and a\n   205\t        // counter that only knows this process's own appends would let anything else that wrote to\n   206\t        // the trail push it past the guard unnoticed. Attributes are read for this path, never for\n   207\t        // whatever a link at this path happens to point at.\n   208\t        bytes = Files.readAttributes(file, BasicFileAttributes.class, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS).size();\n   209\t    }\n   210\t\n   211\t    private static void field(StringBuilder out, String key, String value, boolean first) {\n   212\t        if (!first) {\n   213\t            out.append(',');\n   214\t        }\n   215\t        out.append('\"').append(safeKey(key)).append(\"\\\":\\\"\").append(escape(value)).append('\"');\n   216\t    }\n   217\t\n   218\t    private static String safeKey(String key) {\n   219\t        StringBuilder out = new StringBuilder(key.length());\n   220\t        for (int i = 0; i < key.length(); i++) {\n   221\t            char c = key.charAt(i);\n   222\t            out.append(Character.isLetterOrDigit(c) || c == '_' ? c : '_');\n   223\t        }\n   224\t        return out.toString();\n   225\t    }\n   226\t\n   227\t    /**\n   228\t     * Values are code-supplied, but they are still escaped and length-bounded so a future caller\n   229\t     * cannot break out of the JSON string or write a megabyte per event.\n   230\t     */\n   231\t    private static String escape(String value) {\n   232\t        String bounded = value == null ? \"\" : (value.length() > 256 ? value.substring(0, 256) : value);\n   233\t        StringBuilder out = new StringBuilder(bounded.length() + 8);\n   234\t        for (int i = 0; i < bounded.length(); i++) {\n   235\t            char c = bounded.charAt(i);\n   236\t            switch (c) {\n   237\t                case '\"' -> out.append(\"\\\\\\\"\");\n   238\t                case '\\\\' -> out.append(\"\\\\\\\\\");\n   239\t                case '\\n' -> out.append(\"\\\\n\");\n   240\t                case '\\r' -> out.append(\"\\\\r\");\n   241\t                case '\\t' -> out.append(\"\\\\t\");\n   242\t                default -> {\n   243\t                    if (c < 0x20 || c == 0x7f) {\n   244\t                        out.append(String.format(\"\\\\u%04x\", (int) c));\n   245\t                    } else {\n   246\t                        out.append(c);\n   247\t                    }\n   248\t                }\n   249\t            }\n   250\t        }\n   251\t        return out.toString();\n   252\t    }\n   253\t\n   254\t    /** Reads the trail, for tests and operator inspection. */\n   255\t    public List<String> readAll() throws IOException {\n   256\t        if (!Files.exists(file, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS)) {\n   257\t            return new ArrayList<>();\n   258\t        }\n   259\t        return Files.readAllLines(file, StandardCharsets.UTF_8);\n   260\t    }\n   261\t\n   262\t    public Path path() {\n   263\t        return file;\n   264\t    }\n   265\t\n   266\t    @Override\n   267\t    public void close() {\n   268\t        // Records open the file per append so a long-lived handle cannot be redirected or held\n   269\t        // open by another process; there is nothing to close.\n   270\t    }\n   271\t\n   272\t    /** Audit failure. Callers that gate on auditing must treat this as a denial. */\n   273\t    public static final class AuditException extends Exception {\n   274\t        private static final long serialVersionUID = 1L;\n   275\t\n   276\t        AuditException(String safeDescription) {\n   277\t            super(safeDescription);\n   278\t        }\n   279\t    }\n   280\t}\n===== AuthService.java =====\n     1\tpackage app;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.sql.Connection;\n     4\timport java.sql.PreparedStatement;\n     5\timport java.sql.ResultSet;\n     6\timport java.sql.SQLException;\n     7\timport java.time.Clock;\n     8\t\n     9\t/**\n    10\t * Sign-in: validates a credential against server-side state and issues a session.\n    11\t *\n    12\t * <h2>Uniform failure</h2>\n    13\t * The four ways a sign-in can fail - malformed input, unknown account, wrong password, account\n    14\t * in its lockout window - all answer with {@link #INVALID_MESSAGE} and all perform\n    15\t * <em>exactly one</em> Argon2id derivation. The unknown-account and locked-account paths derive\n    16\t * against {@link #dummyHash()}: the same memory-hard work against a constant credential that can\n    17\t * never match, so neither wall-clock time nor peak memory reveals which branch ran. There is no\n    18\t * sleep-based pad, so the padding cannot drift out of step with the configured cost. A successful\n    19\t * sign-in on an account whose stored parameters are below policy does one extra derivation to\n    20\t * upgrade it - a path that already required the correct password.\n    21\t *\n    22\t * <h2>Atomicity</h2>\n    23\t * One transaction per attempt, in a fixed order: consume the source budget, read the account, take\n    24\t * the {@code auth_guard} row lock, verify, then either clear or charge the account counter and\n    25\t * insert the session. Any failure rolls the whole attempt back, so a session cannot exist without\n    26\t * its account, and a failure cannot be charged to an account without the source budget also being\n    27\t * spent.\n    28\t *\n    29\t * <h2>Lockout is not a denial-of-access lever</h2>\n    30\t * The per-account counter is temporary ({@link Throttle#ACCOUNT_LOCK_SECONDS}) and self-clearing,\n    31\t * and a successful sign-in clears it, so knowing a colleague's email does not let an attacker keep\n    32\t * them out beyond one window. Total attempt rate is bounded by the per-source budget, which is\n    33\t * independent of the account: burning an account's five strikes costs the attacker five of their\n    34\t * own ten.\n    35\t */\n    36\tpublic final class AuthService {\n    37\t\n    38\t    /** Single sign-in refusal for every failure mode, including a locked account. */\n    39\t    public static final String INVALID_MESSAGE = \"The email or password is not correct.\";\n    40\t\n    41\t    /** Shown when the audit trail or the store cannot be honoured: access is refused. */\n    42\t    public static final String INTERNAL_MESSAGE = \"Sign-in is unavailable right now. Try again later.\";\n    43\t\n    44\t    private final AccountStore accounts;\n    45\t    private final Throttle throttle;\n    46\t    private final SessionStore sessions;\n    47\t    private final PasswordHasher hasher;\n    48\t    private final Gate gate;\n    49\t    private final Clock clock;\n    50\t    private final String dummyHash;\n    51\t\n    52\t    /**\n    53\t     * Fixed internal buffer used to buy an equal-cost refusal. Argon2id's cost is set by its\n    54\t     * parameters, not by the length of the input, so a refusal can match the price of a real\n    55\t     * comparison without ever feeding an attacker-sized candidate into the KDF.\n    56\t     */\n    57\t    private static final char[] WORK_FILLER = \"equalising-derivation\".toCharArray();\n    58\t\n    59\t    public AuthService(AccountStore accounts, Throttle throttle, SessionStore sessions,\n    60\t        PasswordHasher hasher, Gate gate, Clock clock) {\n    61\t        this.accounts = accounts;\n    62\t        this.throttle = throttle;\n    63\t        this.sessions = sessions;\n    64\t        this.hasher = hasher;\n    65\t        this.gate = gate;\n    66\t        this.clock = clock;\n    67\t        // Derived once at construction: a genuine Argon2id string with a random salt whose\n    68\t        // password is random and kept nowhere, so nobody - including an operator with the\n    69\t        // database - can present it.\n    70\t        java.security.SecureRandom filler = new java.security.SecureRandom();\n    71\t        char[] throwaway = new char[24];\n    72\t        try {\n    73\t            for (int i = 0; i < throwaway.length; i++) {\n    74\t                throwaway[i] = (char) ('a' + filler.nextInt(26));\n    75\t            }\n    76\t            this.dummyHash = hasher.encode(throwaway);\n    77\t        } finally {\n    78\t            java.util.Arrays.fill(throwaway, '\\0');\n    79\t        }\n    80\t    }\n    81\t\n    82\t    public enum Status {\n    83\t        /** Credential accepted; a session was issued. */\n    84\t        AUTHENTICATED,\n    85\t        /** Generic refusal: malformed input, unknown account, or wrong password. */\n    86\t        INVALID_CREDENTIALS,\n    87\t        /** Refused by a rate limit; the client-visible message is identical to a bad password. */\n    88\t        THROTTLED,\n    89\t        /** Refused because a security control (audit trail or store) could not be honoured. */\n    90\t        UNAVAILABLE\n    91\t    }\n    92\t\n    93\t    public record Result(Status status, String message, SessionStore.Session session) {\n    94\t        public boolean authenticated() {\n    95\t            return status == Status.AUTHENTICATED;\n    96\t        }\n    97\t    }\n    98\t\n    99\t    /**\n   100\t     * @param identifierChars email or username exactly as typed; never trimmed here\n   101\t     * @param passwordChars   candidate password; the caller erases it, this class never stores it\n   102\t     * @param sourceId        opaque caller-supplied source key (a digest in the CLI)\n   103\t     */\n   104\t    public Result login(char[] identifierChars, char[] passwordChars, String sourceId) {\n   105\t        InputPolicy.Identity identity;\n   106\t        try {\n   107\t            identity = InputPolicy.identifier(identifierChars);\n   108\t        } catch (InputPolicy.ValidationException invalidInput) {\n   109\t            return refuseWithoutStore(sourceId, \"identifier rejected\");\n   110\t        }\n   111\t        if (passwordChars == null || passwordChars.length == 0\n   112\t            || passwordChars.length > InputPolicy.PASSWORD_MAX) {\n   113\t            // Never enter the KDF with an attacker-sized buffer: the work factor would be theirs.\n   114\t            // The equalising derivation below uses this class's own fixed buffer instead.\n   115\t            return refuseWithoutStore(sourceId, \"password length rejected\");\n   116\t        }\n   117\t        try {\n   118\t            return attempt(identity, passwordChars, sourceId);\n   119\t        } catch (SQLException | AuditLog.AuditException storeOrAuditFault) {\n   120\t            // Fail closed: an unreadable store or an unwritable trail is a denial, not a bypass.\n   121\t            refuseQuietly(AuditLog.Event.LOGIN_STORE_ERROR, sourceId, \"store or audit fault\");\n   122\t            return new Result(Status.UNAVAILABLE, INTERNAL_MESSAGE, null);\n   123\t        }\n   124\t    }\n   125\t\n   126\t    /**\n   127\t     * Refuses a sign-in whose input was rejected before any credential could be compared. The source\n   128\t     * budget is still charged, and a spent budget still returns the same generic line: otherwise\n   129\t     * sending malformed input would be free - an attacker could force a full key-derivation and an\n   130\t     * audit record on every attempt, forever, without ever touching the limit that is meant to bound\n   131\t     * online work. Charging here is what makes the per-source limit bound the expensive path, not only\n   132\t     * the well-formed one.\n   133\t     */\n   134\t    private Result refuseWithoutStore(String sourceId, String reason) {\n   135\t        try {\n   136\t            boolean allowed = Boolean.TRUE.equals(accounts.db().inTransaction(\n   137\t                connection -> throttle.tryConsumeSource(connection, Throttle.LOGIN_SOURCE_SCOPE, sourceId)));\n   138\t            if (!allowed) {\n   139\t                gate.record(AuditLog.Event.LOGIN_THROTTLED_SOURCE, \"source\", gate.sourceTag(sourceId));\n   140\t                equaliseWork();\n   141\t                return throttled();\n   142\t            }\n   143\t            refuseQuietly(AuditLog.Event.LOGIN_REFUSED_INVALID_INPUT, sourceId, reason);\n   144\t        } catch (SQLException | AuditLog.AuditException storeOrAuditFault) {\n   145\t            // The budget could not be consulted, so the attempt cannot be counted. Fail closed with\n   146\t            // the generic unavailable answer rather than serve a free derivation that escapes the limit.\n   147\t            refuseQuietly(AuditLog.Event.LOGIN_STORE_ERROR, sourceId, \"store or audit fault on refusal\");\n   148\t            return new Result(Status.UNAVAILABLE, INTERNAL_MESSAGE, null);\n   149\t        }\n   150\t        equaliseWork();\n   151\t        return new Result(Status.INVALID_CREDENTIALS, INVALID_MESSAGE, null);\n   152\t    }\n   153\t\n   154\t    /**\n   155\t     * Buys one derivation so a refusal that never compared against the stored credential still\n   156\t     * costs what one that did. Without this, response time alone separates \"no such account\" and\n   157\t     * \"you are being rate limited\" from a wrong password, which is exactly the disclosure the\n   158\t     * uniform message was written to prevent.\n   159\t     */\n   160\t    private void equaliseWork() {\n   161\t        hasher.verify(WORK_FILLER.clone(), dummyHash);\n   162\t    }\n   163\t\n   164\t    private Result attempt(InputPolicy.Identity identity, char[] password, String sourceId)\n   165\t        throws SQLException, AuditLog.AuditException {\n   166\t        final Result[] outcome = new Result[1];\n   167\t        // Set by whichever branch performs a real comparison, so the caller can tell a refusal that\n   168\t        // already cost a derivation from one that cost none. The equalising derivation then runs\n   169\t        // <em>after</em> the transaction closes - never inside it - because the store monitor is\n   170\t        // held for the whole transaction and a padded derivation there would serialise every\n   171\t        // concurrent request behind it.\n   172\t        final boolean[] paidForWork = new boolean[1];\n   173\t        accounts.db().inTransaction(connection -> {\n   174\t            if (!throttle.tryConsumeSource(connection, Throttle.LOGIN_SOURCE_SCOPE, sourceId)) {\n   175\t                gate.record(AuditLog.Event.LOGIN_THROTTLED_SOURCE, \"source\", gate.sourceTag(sourceId));\n   176\t                outcome[0] = throttled();\n   177\t                return Boolean.FALSE;\n   178\t            }\n   179\t\n   180\t            AccountStore.Account account = identity.byEmail()\n   181\t                ? accountBy(connection, \"email = ?\", identity.value())\n   182\t                : accountBy(connection, \"username = ?\", identity.value());\n   183\t\n   184\t            if (account == null) {\n   185\t                paidForWork[0] = true;\n   186\t                hasher.verify(password, dummyHash);\n   187\t                gate.record(AuditLog.Event.LOGIN_FAILURE, \"reason\", \"no such account\",\n   188\t                    \"source\", gate.sourceTag(sourceId),\n   189\t                    \"identity\", identity.byEmail() ? gate.emailTag(identity.value())\n   190\t                        : gate.usernameTag(identity.value()));\n   191\t                outcome[0] = invalid();\n   192\t                return Boolean.FALSE;\n   193\t            }\n   194\t\n   195\t            Throttle.AccountGuard guard = throttle.lockAccountGuard(connection, account.id());\n   196\t            if (guard.isBlocked(clock.instant().getEpochSecond())) {\n   197\t                paidForWork[0] = true;\n   198\t                hasher.verify(password, dummyHash);\n   199\t                gate.record(AuditLog.Event.LOGIN_THROTTLED_ACCOUNT, \"account\", gate.accountTag(account.id()),\n   200\t                    \"source\", gate.sourceTag(sourceId));\n   201\t                outcome[0] = throttled();\n   202\t                return Boolean.FALSE;\n   203\t            }\n   204\t\n   205\t            paidForWork[0] = true;\n   206\t            if (!hasher.verify(password, account.passwordHash())) {\n   207\t                long blockedUntil = throttle.chargeFailure(connection, account.id(), guard);\n   208\t                gate.record(AuditLog.Event.LOGIN_FAILURE, \"reason\", \"credential mismatch\",\n   209\t                    \"account\", gate.accountTag(account.id()), \"source\", gate.sourceTag(sourceId),\n   210\t                    \"locked\", blockedUntil > 0 ? \"yes\" : \"no\");\n   211\t                if (blockedUntil > 0) {\n   212\t                    gate.record(AuditLog.Event.ACCOUNT_LOCKED, \"account\", gate.accountTag(account.id()),\n   213\t                        \"seconds\", Integer.toString(Throttle.ACCOUNT_LOCK_SECONDS));\n   214\t                }\n   215\t                outcome[0] = invalid();\n   216\t                return Boolean.FALSE;\n   217\t            }\n   218\t\n   219\t            throttle.clearFailures(connection, account.id());\n   220\t            if (guard.failures() > 0) {\n   221\t                // The counter was non-zero, so this sign-in ended a strike window: recording that\n   222\t                // lets an operator see a lockout was cleared by the real owner.\n   223\t                gate.record(AuditLog.Event.ACCOUNT_UNLOCKED, \"account\", gate.accountTag(account.id()),\n   224\t                    \"failures\", Integer.toString(guard.failures()));\n   225\t            }\n   226\t            if (hasher.weakerThanPolicy(account.passwordHash())) {\n   227\t                accounts.upgradePasswordHash(account.id(), password, account.passwordUpdatedAt());\n   228\t            }\n   229\t            SessionStore.Session session = sessions.issue(account.id());\n   230\t            gate.record(AuditLog.Event.LOGIN_SUCCESS, \"account\", gate.accountTag(account.id()),\n   231\t                \"source\", gate.sourceTag(sourceId), \"session\", gate.sessionTag(session.token()));\n   232\t            outcome[0] = new Result(Status.AUTHENTICATED, \"Sign-in complete.\", session);\n   233\t            return Boolean.TRUE;\n   234\t        });\n   235\t        if (outcome[0] == null) {\n   236\t            throw new SQLException(\"the sign-in transaction produced no outcome\");\n   237\t        }\n   238\t        if (!paidForWork[0]) {\n   239\t            // Reached by the rate-limited branch, which never read a credential. Pay the same\n   240\t            // price now, outside the store lock, so a throttle cannot be spotted by timing.\n   241\t            equaliseWork();\n   242\t        }\n   243\t        return outcome[0];\n   244\t    }\n   245\t\n   246\t    private static Result invalid() {\n   247\t        return new Result(Status.INVALID_CREDENTIALS, INVALID_MESSAGE, null);\n   248\t    }\n   249\t\n   250\t    private static Result throttled() {\n   251\t        // Deliberately the same wording as a wrong password: a locked account must not be\n   252\t        // distinguishable by the message either. The distinction is recorded in the audit trail.\n   253\t        return new Result(Status.THROTTLED, INVALID_MESSAGE, null);\n   254\t    }\n   255\t\n   256\t    private void refuseQuietly(AuditLog.Event event, String sourceId, String reason) {\n   257\t        try {\n   258\t            gate.record(event, \"reason\", reason, \"source\", gate.sourceTag(sourceId));\n   259\t        } catch (AuditLog.AuditException auditUnavailable) {\n   260\t            // The refusal has already happened and is the safe direction; this path never touched\n   261\t            // a credential, so there is nothing further to protect and no second channel to hide.\n   262\t            System.err.println(\"NOTE an audit record for a refused request could not be written\");\n   263\t        }\n   264\t    }\n   265\t\n   266\t    private static AccountStore.Account accountBy(Connection connection, String predicate, String value)\n   267\t        throws SQLException {\n   268\t        String sql = \"SELECT id, email, username, password_hash, kdf_memory_kib, kdf_iterations,\"\n   269\t            + \" kdf_parallelism, password_updated_at FROM accounts WHERE \" + predicate;\n   270\t        try (PreparedStatement query = connection.prepareStatement(sql)) {\n   271\t            query.setString(1, value);\n   272\t            try (ResultSet found = query.executeQuery()) {\n   273\t                if (!found.next()) {\n   274\t                    return null;\n   275\t                }\n   276\t                return new AccountStore.Account(found.getLong(1), found.getString(2), found.getString(3),\n   277\t                    found.getString(4), found.getInt(5), found.getInt(6), found.getInt(7), found.getLong(8));\n   278\t            }\n   279\t        }\n   280\t    }\n   281\t\n   282\t    /** Validates a session token and resolves it to an account id, or {@code null}. */\n   283\t    public Long resolveSession(String token) {\n   284\t        try {\n   285\t            Long accountId = sessions.resolve(token);\n   286\t            if (accountId == null) {\n   287\t                gate.record(AuditLog.Event.SESSION_REJECTED, \"session\", gate.sessionTag(token));\n   288\t            }\n   289\t            return accountId;\n   290\t        } catch (SQLException | AuditLog.AuditException fault) {\n   291\t            return null;\n   292\t        }\n   293\t    }\n   294\t\n   295\t    /**\n   296\t     * Revokes the presented session. The result distinguishes \"a store/audit fault happened\" from\n   297\t     * the two client-indistinguishable outcomes, so the caller can fail closed on a fault while\n   298\t     * keeping a revoked session and a never-existing one worded identically.\n   299\t     */\n   300\t    public enum LogoutStatus {\n   301\t        /** A live session was ended by this call. */\n   302\t        REVOKED,\n   303\t        /** The token was well-formed but matched no live session; never disclosed as distinct. */\n   304\t        NO_LIVE_SESSION,\n   305\t        /** The store or trail could not be consulted: revocation is <em>not</em> confirmed. */\n   306\t        UNAVAILABLE\n   307\t    }\n   308\t\n   309\t    /**\n   310\t     * Revokes the presented session. The client-visible outcome is kept uniform between\n   311\t     * {@link LogoutStatus#REVOKED} and {@link LogoutStatus#NO_LIVE_SESSION} by the caller, so this\n   312\t     * cannot probe session identifiers - but {@link LogoutStatus#UNAVAILABLE} is reported honestly:\n   313\t     * a bearer credential whose revocation the store could not confirm must not be reported as\n   314\t     * revoked, because that is the one case where a captured token would silently stay live.\n   315\t     * The record is written by {@link SessionStore#revoke}, the layer that actually ends the\n   316\t     * session, so no caller can end one without it appearing in the trail.\n   317\t     */\n   318\t    public LogoutStatus logout(String token) {\n   319\t        try {\n   320\t            return sessions.revoke(token) ? LogoutStatus.REVOKED : LogoutStatus.NO_LIVE_SESSION;\n   321\t        } catch (SQLException | AuditLog.AuditException fault) {\n   322\t            return LogoutStatus.UNAVAILABLE;\n   323\t        }\n   324\t    }\n   325\t\n   326\t    /** The constant credential used to equalise work across failure branches. */\n   327\t    public String dummyHash() {\n   328\t        return dummyHash;\n   329\t    }\n   330\t}\n===== Config.java =====\n     1\tpackage app;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.io.IOException;\n     4\timport java.nio.file.FileVisitResult;\n     5\timport java.nio.file.Files;\n     6\timport java.nio.file.LinkOption;\n     7\timport java.nio.file.Path;\n     8\timport java.nio.file.Paths;\n     9\timport java.nio.file.SimpleFileVisitor;\n    10\timport java.nio.file.attribute.BasicFileAttributes;\n    11\timport java.nio.file.attribute.PosixFilePermission;\n    12\timport java.nio.file.attribute.PosixFilePermissions;\n    13\timport java.sql.Connection;\n    14\timport java.sql.DriverManager;\n    15\timport java.sql.SQLException;\n    16\timport java.util.EnumSet;\n    17\timport java.util.Properties;\n    18\timport java.util.Set;\n    19\t\n    20\t/**\n    21\t * Runtime configuration. Every value here comes from the operator's environment or from an\n    22\t * out-of-argv command-line option; no credential is ever read from a file that this program\n    23\t * created, and no credential has a default value.\n    24\t *\n    25\t * <p>Consumed inputs (all attacker-reachable if the operator's environment is):\n    26\t * <ul>\n    27\t *   <li>{@code APP_DATA_DIR} (or {@code --data-dir}, which must be a separate argv element so\n    28\t *       the path cannot be confused with a password) - key-derivation tuning, each bounded below by an OWASP-equivalent minimum work factor</li>\n    33\t * </ul>\n    34\t * Any of these that is malformed causes a hard stop: there is no permissive default for a\n    35\t * security parameter.\n    36\t */\n    37\tpublic final class Config implements AutoCloseable {\n    38\t\n    39\t    public static final String ENV_DATA_DIR = \"APP_DATA_DIR\";\n    40\t    public static final String ENV_DB_PASSWORD = \"APP_DB_PASSWORD\";\n    41\t    public static final String ENV_DB_PASSWORD_FILE = \"APP_DB_PASSWORD_FILE\";\n    42\t    public static final String ENV_ARGON2_MEMORY = \"APP_ARGON2_MEMORY_KIB\";\n    43\t    public static final String ENV_ARGON2_ITERATIONS = \"APP_ARGON2_ITERATIONS\";\n    44\t    public static final String ENV_ARGON2_PARALLELISM = \"APP_ARGON2_PARALLELISM\";\n    45\t\n    46\t    /**\n    47\t     * Key-derivation defaults. 64 MiB with t=3, p=1 is above the OWASP 2024 Argon2id floor\n    48\t     * (m=19456, t=2, p=1) and measured at roughly 320 ms per hash on this machine, which is\n    49\t     * cheap enough to apply to every login attempt and expensive enough to matter offline.\n    50\t     * Spring Security's own default (16 MiB, t=2) is *below* that floor, so it is not used.\n    51\t     */\n    52\t    public static final int DEFAULT_MEMORY_KIB = 65_536;\n    53\t    public static final int DEFAULT_ITERATIONS = 3;\n    54\t    public static final int DEFAULT_PARALLELISM = 1;\n    55\t\n    56\t    /**\n    57\t     * Configuration bounds are the same bounds {@link PasswordHasher} applies to a stored\n    58\t     * credential, referenced rather than repeated. Two independent numbers for one policy is how a\n    59\t     * legal configuration ends up refused by the schema later; the hasher is the layer that actually\n    60\t     * spends the memory, so it decides the range and the environment can only narrow inside it.\n    61\t     */\n    62\t    private static final int MIN_MEMORY_KIB = PasswordHasher.STORED_MIN_MEMORY_KIB;\n    63\t    private static final int MIN_ITERATIONS = PasswordHasher.STORED_MIN_ITERATIONS;\n    64\t    private static final int MAX_MEMORY_KIB = PasswordHasher.STORED_MAX_MEMORY_KIB;\n    65\t    private static final int MAX_ITERATIONS = PasswordHasher.STORED_MAX_ITERATIONS;\n    66\t\n    67\t    private static final int PASSWORD_MAX_BYTES = 1_048_576;\n    68\t\n    69\t    /** H2 keeps no trace file (which can PosixFilePermission.OWNER_READ, PosixFilePermission.OWNER_WRITE, PosixFilePermission.OWNER_EXECUTE);\n    77\t\n    78\t    private final Path dataDir;\n    79\t    private final Path dbBase;\n  * Opens the database. 147\t    public String jdbcUrl() {\n   148\t        return \"jdbc:h2:file:\" + dbBase + DB_OPTIONS;\n   149\t    }\n   150\t\n   151\t    public Path dataDir() {\n   152\t        return dataDir;\n     return memoryKib;\n   161\t    }\n   162\t\n   163\t    public int argon2Iterations() {\n   164\t        return iterations;\n   165\t    }\n   166\t\n   167\t    public int argon2Parallelism() {\n
