AccountStore.RegisterStatus.THROTTLED ? REFUSED : OK;\n   229\t        } finally {\n   230\t            Arrays.fill(passwordChars, '\\0');\n   231\t            Arrays.fill(emailChars, '\\0');\n   232\t            Arrays.fill(usernameChars, '\\0');\n   233\t        }\n   234\t    }\n   235\t\n   236\t    private int login(AuthService auth) throws IOException {\n   237\t        char[] identifierChars;\n   238\t        char[] passwordChars;\n   239\t        try {\n   240\t            identifierChars = reader.readLine(\"Email or username: \");\n   241\t            passwordChars = reader.readSecret(\"Password: \");\n   242\t        } catch (InputTooLong oversized) {\n   243\t            out.println(AuthService.INVALID_MESSAGE);\n   244\t            recordQuietly(AuditLog.Event.LOGIN_REFUSED_INVALID_INPUT, \"input line too long\");\n   245\t            return REFUSED;\n   246\t        }\n   247\t        try {\n   248\t            AuthService.Result result = auth.login(identifierChars, passwordChars, sourceKey(\"login\"));\n   249\t            out.println(result.message());\n   250\t            if (!result.authenticated()) {\n   251\t                return result.status() == AuthService.Status.UNAVAILABLE ? UNAVAILABLE : REFUSED;\n   252\t            }\n   253\t            String cookie = CookiePolicy.sessionCookie(result.session().token(),\n   254\t                result.session().expiresAt(), java.time.Instant.now().getEpochSecond(), \"/\");\n   255\t            out.println(\"Set-Cookie: \" + cookie);\n   256\t            return OK;\n   257\t        } finally {\n   258\t            Arrays.fill(passwordChars, '\\0');\n   259\t            Arrays.fill(identifierChars, '\\0');\n   260\t        }\n   261\t    }\n   262\t\n   263\t    /**\n   264\t     * The session handle is read from standard input, never taken as an argument: {@code verify}\n   265\t     * and {@code logout} receive a bearer credential, and a value in argv is visible to every other\n   266\t     * user on the machine in a process listing. A leftover {@code <cookie>} operand is refused with\n   267\t     * the generic invalid-session answer rather than being used or echoed.\n   268\t     */\n   269\t    private int verify(String[] operands, AuthService auth, Reader reader) throws IOException {\n   270\t        Long accountId = auth.resolveSession(sessionHandleFrom(operands, reader));\n   271\t        if (accountId == null) {\n   272\t            out.println(\"The session is not valid.\");\n   273\t            return REFUSED;\n   274\t        }\n   275\t        out.println(\"The session is valid.\");\n   276\t        return OK;\n   277\t    }\n   278\t\n   279\t    private int logout(String[] operands, AuthService auth, Reader reader) throws IOException {\n   280\t        String token = sessionHandleFrom(operands, reader);\n   281\t        if (token == null) {\n   282\t            // No usable handle: it was given as an argument, the input ended, or it was malformed.\n   283\t            // Nothing was revoked, so this must not claim the session is gone - the whole point of\n   284\t            // sign-out is that a captured token stops working, and reporting a revocation that never\n   285\t            // happened is exactly the failure that leaves a stolen bearer credential live.\n   286\t            out.println(\"The session is not valid.\");\n   287\t            return REFUSED;\n   288\t        }\n   289\t        AuthService.LogoutStatus status = auth.logout(token);\n   290\t        if (status == AuthService.LogoutStatus.UNAVAILABLE) {\n   291\t            // The store could not confirm the revocation. Report unavailability and do not emit the\n   292\t            // expired cookie, whose Max-Age=0 would tell the client the credential is dead when the\n   293\t            // server has not actually proven that.\n   294\t            err.println(AuthService.INTERNAL_MESSAGE);\n   295\t            return UNAVAILABLE;\n   296\t        }\n   297\t        // REVOKED and NO_LIVE_SESSION are worded identically, so this cannot probe session identifiers.\n   298\t        out.println(\"Sign-out is complete.\");\n   299\t        out.println(\"Set-Cookie: \" + CookiePolicy.expiredSessionCookie(\"/\"));\n   300\t        return OK;\n   301\t    }\n   302\t\n   303\t    private int revokeSessions(AccountStore accounts, SessionStore sessions)\n   304\t        throws IOException, SQLException, AuditLog.AuditException {\n   305\t        char[] usernameChars;\n   306\t        try {\n   307\t            usernameChars = reader.readLine(\"Username: \");\n   308\t        } catch (InputTooLong oversized) {\n   309\t            out.println(\"Sign-out is complete.\");\n   310\t            recordQuietly(AuditLog.Event.ADMIN_REVOKE_REFUSED_INVALID_INPUT, \"input line too long\");\n   311\t            return REFUSED;\n   312\t        }\n   313\t        try {\n   314\t            String username;\n   315\t            try {\n   316\t                username = InputPolicy.username(usernameChars);\n   317\t            } catch (InputPolicy.ValidationException invalid) {\n   318\t                // Same wording as the success path: this command must not reveal whether an\n   319\t                // account exists either. The event names the operation that was refused, not\n   320\t                // registration - a trail that labelled an admin refusal as a sign-up would send\n   321\t                // whoever reads it looking for the wrong attack.\n   322\t                out.println(\"Sign-out is complete.\");\n   323\t                recordQuietly(AuditLog.Event.ADMIN_REVOKE_REFUSED_INVALID_INPUT, invalid.getMessage());\n   324\t                return REFUSED;\n   325\t            }\n   326\t            AccountStore.Account account = accounts.findByUsername(username);\n   327\t            int revoked = account == null ? 0 : sessions.revokeAllForAccount(account.id());\n   328\t            // The operator action is recorded here; SessionStore records the sessions it actually\n   329\t            // ended. Both are needed: one names the request, the other names the effect.\n   330\t            gate.record(AuditLog.Event.ADMIN_REVOKE_REQUESTED,\n   331\t                \"username\", gate.usernameTag(username),\n   332\t                \"account\", account == null ? \"none\" : gate.accountTag(account.id()),\n   333\t                \"count\", Integer.toString(revoked));\n   334\t            out.println(\"Sign-out is complete.\");\n   335\t            return OK;\n   336\t        } finally {\n   337\t            Arrays.fill(usernameChars, '\\0');\n   338\t        }\n   339\t    }\n   340\t\n   341\t    // ------------------------------------------------------------------ helpers\n   342\t\n   343\t    private void recordQuietly(AuditLog.Event event, String reason) {\n   344\t        try {\n   345\t            gate.record(event, \"reason\", reason);\n   346\t        } catch (AuditLog.AuditException auditUnwritable) {\n   347\t            // This path has already refused the request, which is the safe direction, and never\n   348\t            // touched a credential; the note tells the operator the trail is incomplete.\n   349\t            err.println(\"NOTE an audit record for a refused request could not be written\");\n   350\t        }\n   351\t    }\n   352\t\n   353\t    /**\n   354\t     * Reads the session handle from standard input, tolerating the {@code name=value} and\n   355\t     * {@code ; attribute} framing a real cookie header carries, and refuses anything whose shape is\n   356\t     * wrong before any lookup happens.\n   357\t     *\n   358\t     * <p>A {@code <cookie>} given as an operand is refused outright rather than used. A bearer\n   359\t     * credential in argv is readable by every other account on the machine through the process\n   360\t     * list, which is exactly the exposure the rest of this class goes out of its way to avoid for\n   361\t     * passwords; the operand form is therefore not supported even though it is the convenient one.\n   362\t     */\n   363\t    private String sessionHandleFrom(String[] operands, Reader reader) {\n   364\t        if (operands.length > 0) {\n   365\t            err.println(\"The session cookie must be typed on standard input, not given as an\"\n   366\t                + \" argument: arguments are visible in other users' process listings.\");\n   367\t            return null;\n   368\t        }\n   369\t        char[] entered;\n   370\t        try {\n   371\t            entered = reader.readSecret(\"Session cookie: \");\n   372\t        } catch (IOException inputEnded) {\n   373\t            // InputTooLong is an IOException: an oversized cookie is the same refusal as none.\n   374\t            return null;\n   375\t        }\n   376\t        try {\n   377\t            String value = new String(entered);\n   378\t            int equals = value.indexOf('=');\n   379\t            if (equals >= 0) {\n   380\t                value = value.substring(equals + 1);\n   381\t            }\n   382\t            int semicolon = value.indexOf(';');\n   383\t            if (semicolon >= 0) {\n   384\t                value = value.substring(0, semicolon);\n   385\t            }\n   386\t            return SessionStore.isWellFormedToken(value) ? value : null;\n   387\t        } finally {\n   388\t            Arrays.fill(entered, '\\0');\n   389\t        }\n   390\t    }\n   391\t\n   392\t    /**\n   393\t     * The per-source key. This deliverable has no network layer, so the only honest source\n   394\t     * identifier is the OS user running the process. It must be a value the caller cannot choose on\n   395\t     * the launch line: {@code System.getProperty(\"user.name\")} and {@code os.name} are both trivially\n   396\t     * forged with {@code -Duser.name=$RANDOM}, which lets anyone who can exec the program mint a\n   397\t     * fresh, full rate-limit bucket per attempt and defeats the per-source half of the limit\n   398\t     * entirely. The effective uid from {@code /proc/self/status} is maintained by the kernel, not by\n   399\t     * the JVM's argument parsing, so it cannot be overridden that way; changing it means becoming\n   400\t     * another user, which is a stronger claim than launching a process.\n   401\t     *\n   402\t     * <p>Where the kernel uid is genuinely unreadable, the fallback is one <em>shared</em> bucket for\n   403\t     * the whole host, never a fresh one: a shared budget can still be exhausted but cannot be reset,\n   404\t     * which is the fail-closed direction for a flood control. A deployment behind a gateway must\n   405\t     * supply the authenticated client or tenant key instead and validate it as opaque\n   406\t     * {@code [A-Za-z0-9._:-]{1,64}} before it is used as a bucket key - it is attacker-reachable and\n   407\t     * must not be able to choose another tenant's bucket. The remaining risk is stated in the README.\n   408\t     */\n   409\t    private static String sourceKey(String scope) {\n   410\t        String principal = kernelPrincipal();\n   411\t        return Digests.sha256Hex((scope + ':' + principal).getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8))\n   412\t            .substring(0, 32);\n   413\t    }\n   414\t\n   415\t    /** Cached once per process: the uid cannot change under a running JVM that did not setuid. */\n   416\t    private static final String KERNEL_PRINCIPAL = readKernelPrincipal();\n   417\t\n   418\t    private static String kernelPrincipal() {\n   419\t        return KERNEL_PRINCIPAL;\n   420\t    }\n   421\t\n   422\t    /**\n   423\t     * Effective uid from {@code /proc/self/status} as {@code uid:<n>}, or the shared-bucket sentinel\n   424\t     * when it cannot be read. Every failure mode resolves to the sentinel; this never throws, because\n   425\t     * a source key that cannot be computed must degrade to a shared budget rather than to none.\n   426\t     */\n   427\t    private static String readKernelPrincipal() {\n   428\t        try {\n   429\t            for (String line : java.nio.file.Files.readAllLines(\n   430\t                java.nio.file.Path.of(\"/proc/self/status\"), java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets.US_ASCII)) {\n   431\t                if (!line.startsWith(\"Uid:\")) {\n   432\t                    continue;\n   433\t                }\n   434\t                String[] fields = line.substring(\"Uid:\".length()).trim().split(\"\\\\s+\");\n   435\t                // real, effective, saved, filesystem - the effective uid is what gates file access.\n   436\t                if (fields.length >= 2 && isAllDigits(fields[1])) {\n   437\t                    return \"uid:\" + fields[1];\n   438\t                }\n   439\t                break;\n   440\t            }\n   441\t        } catch (java.io.IOException | RuntimeException unreadable) {\n   442\t            // fall through to the shared sentinel below\n   443\t        }\n   444\t        return \"shared-local\";\n   445\t    }\n   446\t\n   447\t    private static boolean isAllDigits(String value) {\n   448\t        if (value.isEmpty()) {\n   449\t            return false;\n   450\t        }\n   451\t        for (int i = 0; i < value.length(); i++) {\n   452\t            if (value.charAt(i) < '0' || value.charAt(i) > '9') {\n   453\t                return false;\n   454\t            }\n   455\t        }\n   456\t        return true;\n   457\t    }\n   458\t\n   459\t    private static final String REFUSED_MESSAGE = \"The details supplied are not acceptable.\";\n   460\t\n   461\t    // ------------------------------------------------------------------ input\n   462\t\n   463\t    /**\n   464\t     * Line reader with a hard cap applied during the read. The terminal path uses\n   465\t     * {@code Console.readPassword} for secrets so nothing is echoed; the byte path is reached when\n   466\t     * standard input is a pipe or file, which is how a scripted session and the test suite drive\n   467\t     * the real binary.\n   468\t     */\n   469\t    static final class Reader {\n   470\t\n   471\t        private final InputStream in;\n   472\t        private final Console console;\n   473\t        private final PrintStream promptStream;\n   474\t\n   475\t        Reader(InputStream in, Console console, PrintStream promptStream) {\n   476\t            this.in = in;\n   477\t            this.console = console;\n   478\t            this.promptStream = promptStream;\n   479\t        }\n   480\t\n   481\t        char[] readLine(String promptText) throws IOException {\n   482\t            if (console != null) {\n   483\t                String entered = console.readLine(\"%s\", promptText);\n   484\t                if (entered == null) {\n   485\t                    throw new IOException(\"the input stream ended unexpectedly\");\n   486\t                }\n   487\t                if (entered.length() > InputPolicy.MAX_FIELD_CHARS) {\n   488\t                    throw new InputTooLong();\n   489\t                }\n   490\t                return entered.toCharArray();\n   491\t            }\n   492\t            return readCapped(promptText, InputPolicy.MAX_FIELD_CHARS, true);\n   493\t        }\n   494\t\n   495\t        char[] readSecret(String promptText) throws IOException {\n   496\t            if (console != null) {\n   497\t                char[] entered = console.readPassword(\"%s\", promptText);\n   498\t                if (entered == null) {\n   499\t                    throw new IOException(\"the input stream ended unexpectedly\");\n   500\t                }\n   501\t                return entered;\n   502\t            }\n   503\t            // On a terminal the keystroke that submits the password supplies the line break; on a\n   504\t            // pipe nothing is echoed, so this path ends the prompt line itself. A newline carries no\n   505\t            // information about the secret, and without it the next output line would render as part\n   506\t            // of the prompt.\n   507\t            return readCapped(promptText, InputPolicy.PASSWORD_MAX, true);\n   508\t        }\n   509\t\n   510\t        /** Reads up to {@code max} bytes before the newline; anything longer is refused outright. */\n   511\t        private char[] readCapped(String promptText, int max, boolean echoNewline) throws IOException {\n   512\t            promptStream.print(promptText);\n   513\t            promptStream.flush();\n   514\t            byte[] buffer = new byte[max + 1];\n   515\t            int length = 0;\n   516\t            boolean sawByte = false;\n   517\t            int value;\n   518\t            while ((value = in.read()) >= 0) {\n   519\t                sawByte = true;\n   520\t                if (value == '\\n') {\n   521\t                    break;\n   522\t                }\n   523\t                if (value == '\\r') {\n   524\t                    continue;\n   525\t                }\n   526\t                if (length >= buffer.length) {\n   527\t                    // Cap reached: drain the rest of the line so the next prompt is not fed the\n   528\t                    // tail of an oversized input, erase what we hold, then refuse.\n   529\t                    while ((value = in.read()) >= 0 && value != '\\n') {\n   530\t                        // discarded on purpose\n   531\t                    }\n   532\t                    Arrays.fill(buffer, (byte) 0);\n   533\t                    throw new InputTooLong();\n   534\t                }\n   535\t                buffer[length++] = (byte) value;\n   536\t            }\n   537\t            if (!sawByte) {\n   538\t                throw new IOException(\"the input stream ended unexpectedly\");\n   539\t            }\n   540\t            byte[] exact = Arrays.copyOf(buffer, length);\n   541\t            Arrays.fill(buffer, (byte) 0);\n   542\t            String decoded = new String(exact, StandardCharsets.UTF_8);\n   543\t            Arrays.fill(exact, (byte) 0);\n   544\t            if (echoNewline) {\n   545\t                promptStream.println();\n   546\t            }\n   547\t            return decoded.toCharArray();\n   548\t        }\n   549\t    }\n   550\t\n   551\t    /** Signals an oversized input line; mapped to the same generic refusal as any invalid input. */\n   552\t    static final class InputTooLong extends IOException {\n   553\t        private static final long serialVersionUID = 1L;\n   554\t    }\n   555\t}\n===== Maintenance.java =====\n     1\tpackage app;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.sql.SQLException;\n     4\t\n     5\t/**\n     6\t * Bounds the state that the feature itself creates.\n     7\t *\n     8\t * <p>Every failed sign-in, every distinct source and every issued session writes a row. Without a\n     9\t * sweep the store only grows, and unbounded growth in a security table is its own problem: it\n    10\t * slows the very lookups that decide access and eventually exhausts the disk, which the audit\n    11\t * trail turns into a denial. So the state that can no longer affect a decision is deleted -\n    12\t * expired or revoked sessions, elapsed throttle windows, and guard rows for accounts that are both\n    13\t * outside the recent window <em>and</em> hold no live state.\n    14\t *\n    15\t * <p>A sweep never weakens a control that is still in force. A source bucket is eligible only once\n    16\t * its whole window has elapsed, which is the moment its next attempt would refill it anyway, so\n    17\t * deleting rows cannot be used to reset a denial that is currently blocking somebody. A guard row is\n    18\t * kept for the same reason even when its account has aged out of retention: if its lockout has not\n    19\t * expired, or its failure window is still running, deleting it would hand a rate limit or a lockout\n    20\t * back to whoever triggered the sweep.\n    21\t *\n    22\t * <p>Failure to sweep is reported and ignored, never fatal and never a denial: this is a\n    23\t * housekeeping task, and refusing a sign-in because old rows were not deleted would be a denial of\n    24\t * service caused by maintenance rather than by an attack.\n    25\t */\n    26\tpublic final class Maintenance {\n    27\t\n    28\t    /** Guard rows are kept for this many of the most recently created accounts. */\n    29\t    static final int RECENT_ACCOUNTS_TO_KEEP = 10_000;\n    30\t\n    31\t    private final SessionStore sessions;\n    32\t    private final Throttle throttle;\n    33\t    private final Gate gate;\n    34\t\n    35\t    public Maintenance(SessionStore sessions, Throttle throttle, Gate gate) {\n    36\t        this.sessions = sessions;\n    37\t        this.throttle = throttle;\n    38\t        this.gate = gate;\n    39\t    }\n    40\t\n    41\t    /** Runs one sweep; returns the number of rows removed, which is zero when the sweep failed. */\n    42\t    public int run() {\n    43\t        int sessionsRemoved;\n    44\t        int throttleRemoved;\n    45\t        try {\n    46\t            sessionsRemoved = sessions.purgeExpired();\n    47\t            throttleRemoved = throttle.purgeStaleState(RECENT_ACCOUNTS_TO_KEEP);\n    48\t        } catch (SQLException | AuditLog.AuditException sweepFailed) {\n    49\t            System.err.println(\"NOTE the maintenance sweep could not complete;\"\n    50\t                + \" the operation that triggered it is unaffected\");\n    51\t            return 0;\n    52\t        }\n    53\t        int removed = sessionsRemoved + throttleRemoved;\n    54\t        // Only a sweep that changed state is recorded. This runs on every invocation, so a record per\n    55\t        // no-op would make the trail grow in proportion to how often the program is started - the one\n    56\t        // resource the audit budget exists to bound.\n    57\t        if (removed == 0) {\n    58\t            return 0;\n    59\t        }\n    60\t        try {\n    61\t            gate.record(AuditLog.Event.MAINTENANCE_RUN,\n    62\t                \"sessions\", Integer.toString(sessionsRemoved),\n    63\t                \"throttle\", Integer.toString(throttleRemoved));\n    64\t        } catch (AuditLog.AuditException auditUnwritable) {\n    65\t            // Nothing was denied by this record, so an unwritable trail must not undo a sweep that\n    66\t            // already committed. The state below is already smaller; reporting is all that failed.\n    67\t            System.err.println(\"NOTE the maintenance sweep could not be recorded\");\n    68\t        }\n    69\t        return removed;\n    70\t    }\n    71\t}\n===== PasswordHasher.java =====\n     1\tpackage app;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.security.GeneralSecurityException;\n     4\timport java.security.MessageDigest;\n     5\timport java.security.SecureRandom;\n     6\timport java.util.Arrays;\n     7\timport java.util.Base64;\n     8\timport java.util.Locale;\n     9\timport java.util.regex.Matcher;\n    10\timport java.util.regex.Pattern;\n    11\timport org.bouncycastle.crypto.generators.Argon2BytesGenerator;\n    12\timport org.bouncycastle.crypto.params.Argon2Parameters;\n    13\t\n    14\t/**\n    15\t * Argon2id password hashing (RFC 9106) using the Bouncy Castle primitive directly.\n    16\t *\n    17\t * <p>Why not {@code Argon2PasswordEncoder} from Spring Security, which is the obvious built-in:\n    18\t * <ol>\n    19\t *   <li>its {@code defaultsForSpringSecurity_v5_8()} parameters (m=16 MiB, t=2, p=1) are\n    20\t *       <em>below</em> the OWASP Argon2id cheat-sheet floor of m=19456 KiB, t=2, p=1, and</li>\n    21\t *   <li>{@code matches()} re-derives using the memory/iteration/parallelism values embedded in\n    22\t *       the stored string, so a database row an attacker could rewrite makes this process\n    23\t *       allocate unbounded memory (a stored {@code m=4194304} asks for 4 GiB) before it answers.\n    24\t *       That makes a stored value decide a resource outcome unchecked.</li>\n    25\t * </ol>\n    26\t * Encoding and verification here use the same library and the same RFC-9106 encoding format, and\n    27\t * the produced strings are verified to be readable by Spring's encoder in the test suite, so the\n    28\t * credential format stays standard while the policy on stored values stays ours.\n    29\t *\n    30\t * <p>Every {@code char[]} handed to this class is the caller's; this class never stores it, never\n    31\t * converts it to a {@link String}, and erases only the buffers it allocates itself. Zeroing is\n    32\t * best-effort against heap dumps: Bouncy Castle's internal blocks and the JVM's {@code char[]}\n    33\t * copies cannot be erased from here, which the README states plainly.\n    34\t */\n    35\tpublic final class PasswordHasher {\n    36\t\n    37\t    /** Salt length in bytes, per RFC 9106 (16 bytes minimum for Argon2id). */\n    38\t    static final int SALT_BYTES = 16;\n    39\t    static final int HASH_BYTES = 32;\n    40\t\n    41\t    /**\n    42\t     * Bounds accepted for a hash that is <em>already stored</em>. The lower bound is the OWASP\n    43\t     * Argon2id floor, so a downgraded row is refused rather than verified cheaply; the upper\n    44\t     * bound caps the memory a rewritten row can demand.\n    45\t     */\n    46\t    static final int STORED_MIN_MEMORY_KIB = 19_456;\n    47\t    static final int STORED_MAX_MEMORY_KIB = 1_048_576;\n    48\t    static final int STORED_MIN_ITERATIONS = 2;\n    49\t    static final int STORED_MAX_ITERATIONS = 20;\n    50\t    static final int STORED_MIN_PARALLELISM = 1;\n    51\t    static final int STORED_MAX_PARALLELISM = 8;\n    52\t\n    53\t    /** Canonical PHC form, so no ambiguous or unpadded base64 can denote a stored credential. */\n    54\t    private static final Pattern PHC = Pattern.compile(\n    55\t        \"^\\\\$argon2id\\\\$v=19\\\\$m=([0-9]{1,9}),t=([0-9]{1,4}),p=([0-9]{1,3})\\\\$\"\n    56\t            + \"([A-Za-z0-9+/]{22})\\\\$([A-Za-z0-9+/]{43})$\");\n    57\t\n    58\t    private static final Base64.Encoder B64 = Base64.getEncoder().withoutPadding();\n    59\t    private static final Base64.Decoder B64_STRICT = Base64.getDecoder();\n    60\t\n    61\t    /**\n    62\t     * Longest credential string these bounds can produce, measured rather than guessed: every\n    63\t     * bound is a maximum, and the maximum cost set is {@code m=1048576,t=20,p=8}. The account\n    64\t     * store sizes its credential column from this value, so a configured cost that this class\n    65\t     * accepts can never be refused by the schema on the way in. Deriving it here keeps the writer\n    66\t     * of credentials and the storage that holds them from drifting apart.\n    67\t     */\n    68\t    static final int MAX_STORED_HASH_CHARS =\n    69\t        format(STORED_MAX_MEMORY_KIB, STORED_MAX_ITERATIONS, STORED_MAX_PARALLELISM,\n    70\t            new byte[SALT_BYTES], new byte[HASH_BYTES]).length();\n    71\t\n    72\t    private final SecureRandom random = new SecureRandom();\n    73\t    private final int memoryKib;\n    74\t    private final int iterations;\n    75\t    private final int parallelism;\n    76\t\n    77\t    public PasswordHasher(int memoryKib, int iterations, int parallelism) throws GeneralSecurityException {\n    78\t        if (memoryKib < STORED_MIN_MEMORY_KIB || memoryKib > STORED_MAX_MEMORY_KIB) {\n    79\t            throw new GeneralSecurityException(\"configured Argon2 memory is outside the approved range\");\n    80\t        }\n    81\t        if (iterations < STORED_MIN_ITERATIONS || iterations > STORED_MAX_ITERATIONS) {\n    82\t            throw new GeneralSecurityException(\"configured Argon2 iterations are outside the approved range\");\n    83\t        }\n    84\t        if (parallelism < STORED_MIN_PARALLELISM || parallelism > STORED_MAX_PARALLELISM) {\n    85\t            throw new GeneralSecurityException(\"configured Argon2 parallelism is outside the approved range\");\n    86\t        }\n    87\t        this.memoryKib = memoryKib;\n    88\t        this.iterations = iterations;\n    89\t        this.parallelism = parallelism;\n    90\t    }\n    91\t\n    92\t    /**\n    93\t     * @return {@code $argon2id$v=19$m=..,t=..,p=..$<salt>$<hash>} with a fresh CSPRNG salt\n    94\t     */\n    95\t    public String encode(char[] password) {\n    96\t        if (password == null || password.length == 0) {\n    97\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"a password is required to hash\");\n    98\t        }\n    99\t        byte[] salt = new byte[SALT_BYTES];\n   100\t        random.nextBytes(salt);\n   101\t        byte[] hash = derive(password, salt, memoryKib, iterations, parallelism);\n   102\t        try {\n   103\t            return format(memoryKib, iterations, parallelism, salt, hash);\n   104\t        } finally {\n   105\t            Arrays.fill(hash, (byte) 0);\n   106\t            Arrays.fill(salt, (byte) 0);\n   107\t        }\n   108\t    }\n   109\t\n   110\t    /**\n   111\t     * Verifies a candidate password against a stored hash. Returns {@code false} for a stored\n   112\t     * value that is malformed or outside the approved parameter bounds - the caller cannot tell\n   113\t     * that apart from a wrong password, and neither outcome is granted access.\n   114\t     */\n   115\t    public boolean verify(char[] password, String storedHash) {\n   116\t        if (password == null || password.length == 0 || storedHash == null) {\n   117\t            return false;\n   118\t        }\n   119\t        byte[] salt = null;\n   120\t        byte[] expected = null;\n   121\t        byte[] actual = null;\n   122\t        try {\n   123\t            Matcher shape = PHC.matcher(storedHash);\n   124\t            if (!shape.matches()) {\n   125\t                return false;\n   126\t            }\n   127\t            int memory = Integer.parseInt(shape.group(1));\n   128\t            int iterationsRead = Integer.parseInt(shape.group(2));\n   129\t            int parallelismRead = Integer.parseInt(shape.group(3));\n   130\t            if (memory < STORED_MIN_MEMORY_KIB || memory > STORED_MAX_MEMORY_KIB\n   131\t                || iterationsRead < STORED_MIN_ITERATIONS || iterationsRead > STORED_MAX_ITERATIONS\n   132\t                || parallelismRead < STORED_MIN_PARALLELISM || parallelismRead > STORED_MAX_PARALLELISM) {\n   133\t                return false;\n   134\t            }\n   135\t            salt = decodeCanonical(shape.group(4));\n   136\t            expected = decodeCanonical(shape.group(5));\n   137\t            if (salt == null || expected == null\n   138\t                || salt.length != SALT_BYTES || expected.length != HASH_BYTES) {\n   139\t                return false;\n   140\t            }\n   141\t            actual = derive(password, salt, memory, iterationsRead, parallelismRead);\n   142\t            // Lengths are fixed by the pattern above, so this comparison reveals nothing beyond\n   143\t            // the match result itself.\n   144\t            return {\n   149\t                Arrays.fill(salt, (byte) 0);\n   150\t            }\n   151\t            if (expected != null) {\n   152\t                Arrays.fill(expected, (byte) 0);\n   153\t            }\n   154\t            if (actual != null) {\n   155\t                Arrays.fill(actual, (byte) 0);\n   156\t            }\n   157\t        }\n   158\t    }\n   159\t\n   160\t    /** Cost parameters read back out of a stored hash string. */\n   161\t    public record Cost(int memoryKib, int iterations, int parallelism) {\n   162\t    }\n   163\t\n   164\t    /**\n   165\t     *     return new Cost(Integer.parseInt(shape.group(1)), Integer.parseInt(shape.group(2)),\n   176\t            Integer.parseInt(shape.group(3)));\n   177\t    }\n   178\t\n   179\t    /**\n   180\t */\n   183\t    public boolean weakerThanPolicy(String storedHash) {\n   184\t        if (storedHash == null) {\n   185\t            return true;\n   186\t        }\n   187\t        Matcher shape = PHC.matcher(storedHash);\n   188\t        if (!shape.matches()) {\n   189\t            return true;\n   190\t        }\n   191\t        int memory = Integer.parseInt(shape.group(1));\n   192\t        int iterationsRead = Integer.parseInt(shape.group(2));\n   193\t        int parallelismRead = Integer.parseInt(shape.group(3));\n   194\t        return memory < memoryKib || iterationsRead < iterations || parallelismRead < parallelism;\n   195\t    }\n   196\t\n   197\t    public int policyMemoryKib() {\n   198\t        return memoryKib;\n   199\t    }\n   200\t\n   201\t    public int policyIterations() {\n   202\t        return iterations;\n   203\t    }\n   204\t\n   205\t    public int policyParallelism() {\n   206\t        return parallelism;\n   207\t    }\n   208\t\n   209\t    private static byte[] derive(char[] password, byte[] salt, int memoryKib, int iterations, int parallelism) {\n   210\t        Argon2Parameters parameters = new Argon2Parameters.Builder(Argon2Parameters.ARGON2_id)\n   211\t            .withSalt(salt)\n   212\t            .withParallelism(parallelism)\n   213\t            .withMemoryAsKB(memoryKib)\n   214\t            .withIterations(iterations)\n   215\t            .build();\n   216\t        Argon2BytesGenerator generator = new Argon2BytesGenerator();\n   217\t        generator.init(parameters);\n   218\t        byte[] out = new byte[HASH_BYTES];\n   219\t        generator.generateBytes(password, out);\n   220\t        return out;\n   221\t    }\n   222\t\n   223\t    private static String format(int memory, int iterations, int parallelism, byte[] salt, byte[] hash) {\n   224\t        return \"$argon2id$v=19$m=\" + memory + \",t=\" + iterations + \",p=\" + parallelism + \"$\"\n   225\t            + B64.encodeToString(salt) + \"$\" + B64.encodeToString(hash);\n   226\t    }\n   227\t\n   228\t    /**\n   229\t     * Java's \"basic\" decoder silently accepts unpadded input, so it is not canonical on its own.\n   230\t     * Re-encoding and comparing rejects any alternative spelling of the same bytes, keeping the\n   231\t     * stored credential string a unique representation.\n   232\t     */\n   233\t    private static byte[] decodeCanonical(String text) {\n   234\t        byte[] decoded;\n   235\t        try {\n   236\t            decoded = B64_STRICT.decode(text);\n   237\t        } catch (IllegalArgumentException notBase64) {\n   238\t            return null;\n   239\t        }\n   240\t        return B64.encodeToString(decoded).equals(text) ? decoded : null;\n   241\t    }\n   242\t\n   243\t    /** Exposed for the test suite's cross-check against Spring's encoder. */\n   244\t    static String describe(String storedHash) {\n   245\t        if (storedHash == null) {\n   246\t            return \"null\";\n   247\t        }\n   248\t        Matcher shape = PHC.matcher(storedHash);\n   249\t        if (!shape.matches()) {\n   250\t            return \"malformed\";\n   251\t        }\n   252\t        return String.format(Locale.ROOT, \"m=%s,t=%s,p=%s\", shape.group(1), shape.group(2), shape.group(3));\n   253\t    }\n   254\t}\n===== Secrets.java =====\n     1\tpackage app;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.util.Arrays;\n     4\t\n     5\t/**\n     6\t * A secret (user password, database password, session token) held in a {@code char[]}\n     7\t * so the holder can erase it, with a {@code toString()} that cannot leak it.\n     8\t *\n     9\t * <p>Callers must not copy the array returned by {@link #chars()} into a {@link String}:\n    10\t * Strings are interned in the heap and cannot be zeroed. The one unavoidable exception is\n    11\t * the JDBC {@code Properties} hand-off in {@link Config}, which is documented there.\n    12\t */\n    13\tpublic final class Secrets implements AutoCloseable {\n    14\t\n    15\t    private char[] value;\n    16\t\n    17\t    private Secrets(char[] value) {\n    18\t        this.value = value;\n    19\t    }\n    20\t\n    21\t    /** Takes ownership of {@code value} by copying it and erasing the caller's array. */\n    22\t    public static Secrets ofCharArray(char[] value) {\n    23\t        char[] copy = value.clone();\n    24\t        Arrays.fill(value, '\\0');\n    25\t        return new Secrets(copy);\n    26\t    }\n    27\t\n    28\t    public static Secrets ofString(String value) {\n    29\t        return new Secrets(value == null ? null : value.toCharArray());\n    30\t    }\n    31\t\n    32\t    /** The live characters. Valid only until {@link #close()}; do not retain. */\n    33\t    public char[] chars() {\n    34\t        return value;\n    35\t    }\n    36\t\n    37\t    public int length() {\n    38\t        return value == null ? 0 : value.length;\n    39\t    }\n    40\t\n    41\t    public boolean isBlank() {\n    42\t        if (value == null || value.length == 0) {\n    43\t            return true;\n    44\t        }\n    45\t        for (char c : value) {\n    46\t            if (!Character.isWhitespace(c) && c != '\\0') {\n    47\t                return false;\n    48\t            }\n    49\t        }\n    50\t        return true;\n    51\t    }\n    52\t\n    53\t    /** Overwrites the secret in place. Idempotent. */\n    54\t    @Override\n    55\t    public void close() {\n    56\t        if (value != null) {\n    57\t            Arrays.fill(value, '\\0');\n    58\t            value = null;\n    59\t        }\n    60\t    }\n    61\t\n    62\t    @Override\n    63\t    public String toString() {\n    64\t        return \"Secrets[redacted]\";\n    65\t    }\n    66\t\n    67\t    @Override\n    68\t    public boolean equals(Object other) {\n    69\t        return this == other;\n    70\t    }\n    71\t\n    72\t    @Override\n    73\t    public int hashCode() {\n    74\t        return Secrets.class.getName().hashCode();\n    75\t    }\n    76\t}\n===== SessionStore.java =====\n     1\tpackage app;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.security.SecureRandom;\n     4\timport java.sql.Connection;\n     5\timport java.sql.PreparedStatement;\n     6\timport java.sql.ResultSet;\n     7\timport java.sql.SQLException;\n     8\timport java.time.Clock;\n     9\timport java.util.Base64;\n    10\t\n    11\t/**\n    12\t * Opaque bearer sessions.\n    13\t *\n    14\t * <p>The presented token is 32 bytes from {@link SecureRandom} (256 bits of entropy) rendered as\n    15\t * 64 lower-case hex characters. It is generated here and is never accepted from a caller as an\n    16\t * identity, never derived from an account id, and never predictable: knowing one valid token says\n    17\t * nothing about any other. Hex is chosen over base64 so the presented token and its stored digest\n    18\t * share one alphabet and one {@code CHAR(64)} column, and because every hex character is a valid\n    19\t * RFC 6265 cookie-value octet. Only the SHA-256 of the token is stored, so a database dump yields\n    20\t * no usable credential. Because the stored value is the hash of a uniformly random 256-bit input,\n    21\t * the lookup is an exact indexed match and needs no blind index - the property that would make a\n    22\t * plain hash unsafe (an attacker brute-forcing low-entropy input) does not apply to a 256-bit\n    23\t * random value. A password, which <em>is</em> low-entropy, gets Argon2id with a per-record salt\n    24\t * from {@link PasswordHasher} instead.\n    25\t *\n    26\t * <p>Validation takes a row lock ({@code FOR UPDATE}) so a concurrent revocation cannot be\n    27\t * answered with a stale \"valid\", and the returned identity comes from that row - never from the\n    28\t * token itself.\n    29\t */\n    30\tpublic final class SessionStore {\n    31\t\n    32\t    /** Absolute session lifetime. No sliding extension, so a stolen token has a hard expiry. */\n    33\t    public static final int SESSION_LIFETIME_SECONDS = 8 * 60 * 60;\n    34\t\n    35\t    /** Length of the presented token: 32 random bytes as lower-case hex. */\n    36\t    public static final int TOKEN_CHARS = 64;\n    37\t\n    38\t    private final Db db;\n    39\t    private final Gate gate;\n    40\t    private final Clock clock;\n    41\t    private final SecureRandom random = new SecureRandom();\n    42\t\n    43\t    public SessionStore(Db db, Gate gate, Clock clock) {\n    44\t        this.db = db;\n    45\t        this.gate = gate;\n    46\t        this.clock = clock;\n    47\t    }\n    48\t\n    49\t    public record Session(String token, long accountId, long expiresAt) {\n    50\t    }\n    51\t\n    52\t    /**\n    53\t     * Issues a new session for an account that the caller has already authenticated.\n    54\t     *\n    55\t     * <p>The record is written inside the issuing transaction, so a live session always has a\n    56\t     * trail entry behind it - and only a salted tag is written, never the bearer token itself or\n    57\t     * the SHA-256 that stands for it in the table.\n    58\t     */\n    59\t    public Session issue(long accountId) throws SQLException, AuditLog.AuditException {\n    60\t        byte[] material = new byte[32];\n    61\t        random.nextBytes(material);\n    62\t        String token;\n    63\t        try {\n    64\t            token = Digests.hex(material);\n    65\t        } finally {\n    66\t            java.util.Arrays.fill(material, (byte) 0);\n    67\t        }\n    68\t        if (token.length() != TOKEN_CHARS) {\n    69\t            throw new IllegalStateException(\"unexpected token length\");\n    70\t        }\n    71\t        String tokenHash = Digests.sha256Hex(token.getBytes(java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets.US_ASCII));\n    72\t        long now = clock.instant().getEpochSecond();\n    73\t        long expiresAt = now + SESSION_LIFETIME_SECONDS;\n    74\t        db.inTransaction(connection -> {\n    75\t            // A session may only exist for an account that really exists: the identity in a\n    76\t            // presented token is always resolved against server-side state, never trusted.\n    77\t            try (PreparedStatement check = connection.prepareStatement(\"SELECT id FROM accounts WHERE id = ?\")) {\n    78\t                check.setLong(1, accountId);\n    79\t                try (ResultSet found = check.executeQuery()) {\n    80\t                    if (!found.next()) {\n    81\t                        throw new SQLException(\"cannot issue a session for an unknown account\");\n    82\t                    }\n    83\t                }\n    84\t            }\n    85\t            try (PreparedStatement insert = connection.prepareStatement(\n    86\t                \"INSERT INTO sessions (token_hash, account_id, issued_at, expires_at, revoked_at)\"\n    87\t                    + \" VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, NULL)\")) {\n    88\t                insert.setString(1, tokenHash);\n    89\t                insert.setLong(2, accountId);\n    90\t                insert.setLong(3, now);\n    91\t                insert.setLong(4, expiresAt);\n    92\t                insert.executeUpdate();\n    93\t            }\n    94\t            gate.record(AuditLog.Event.SESSION_ISSUED, \"account\", gate.accountTag(accountId),\n    95\t                \"session\", gate.sessionTag(token), \"expires\", Long.toString(expiresAt));\n    96\t            return Boolean.TRUE;\n    97\t        });\n    98\t        return new Session(token, accountId, expiresAt);\n    99\t    }\n   100\t\n   101\t    /**\n   102\t     * Resolves a presented token to an account id, or {@code null} when it is unknown, expired\n   103\t     * or revoked. Malformed tokens are refused before any query, so a caller cannot probe the\n   104\t     * shape of stored hashes.\n   105\t     */\n   106\t    public Long resolve(String token) throws SQLException, AuditLog.AuditException {\n   107\t        if (!isWellFormedToken(token)) {\n   108\t            return null;\n   109\t        }\n   110\t        String tokenHash = Digests.sha256Hex(token.getBytes(java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets.US_ASCII));\n   111\t        long now = clock.instant().getEpochSecond();\n   112\t        return db.inTransaction(connection -> {\n   113\t            try (PreparedStatement live = connection.prepareStatement(\n   114\t                \"SELECT account_id FROM sessions WHERE token_hash = ? AND revoked_at IS NULL\"\n   115\t                    + \" AND expires_at > ? FOR UPDATE\")) {\n   116\t                live.setString(1, tokenHash);\n   117\t                live.setLong(2, now);\n   118\t                try (ResultSet found = live.executeQuery()) {\n   119\t                    return found.next() ? Long.valueOf(found.getLong(1)) : null;\n   120\t                }\n   121\t            }\n   122\t        });\n   123\t    }\n   124\t\n   125\t    /**\n   126\t     * Revokes a session. Returns {@code true} when a previously live session was revoked by this\n   127\t     * call; the caller reports a generic result either way.\n   128\t     *\n   129\t     * <p>The record is written <em>inside</em> the revoking transaction, exactly as issuance does,\n   130\t     * so an ended session always has a trail entry behind it and a trail that cannot be written\n   131\t     * rolls the revocation back rather than committing an unrecorded state change. That is what\n   132\t     * makes \"the store could not confirm the revocation\" mean <em>nothing changed</em> - the only\n   133\t     * reading under which a caller may honestly refuse to tell a client their token is dead.\n   134\t     */\n   135\t    public boolean revoke(String token) throws SQLException, AuditLog.AuditException {\n   136\t        if (!isWellFormedToken(token)) {\n   137\t            return false;\n   138\t        }\n   139\t        String tokenHash = Digests.sha256Hex(token.getBytes(java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets.US_ASCII));\n   140\t        long now = clock.instant().getEpochSecond();\n   141\t        return Boolean.TRUE.equals(db.inTransaction(connection -> {\n   142\t            try (PreparedStatement revoke = connection.prepareStatement(\n   143\t                \"UPDATE sessions SET revoked_at = ? WHERE token_hash = ? AND revoked_at IS NULL\")) {\n   144\t                revoke.setLong(1, now);\n   145\t                revoke.setString(2, tokenHash);\n   146\t                boolean ended = revoke.executeUpdate() == 1;\n   147\t                if (ended) {\n   148\t                    gate.record(AuditLog.Event.SESSION_REVOKED, \"session\", gate.sessionTag(token));\n   149\t                }\n   150\t                return ended;\n   151\t            }\n   152\t        }));\n   153\t    }\n   154\t\n   155\t    /**\n   156\t     * Revokes every live session belonging to an account, so a credential change or an operator\n   157\t     * lockout cannot leave an already-issued bearer token valid. Recorded inside the transaction\n   158\t     * for the same reason as {@link #revoke}: the recorded count is the count that really ended.\n   159\t     */\n   160\t    public int revokeAllForAccount(long accountId) throws SQLException, AuditLog.AuditException {\n   161\t        long now = clock.instant().getEpochSecond();\n   162\t        return db.inTransaction(connection -> {\n   163\t            try (PreparedStatement revoke = connection.prepareStatement(\n   164\t                \"UPDATE sessions SET revoked_at = ? WHERE account_id = ? AND revoked_at IS NULL\")) {\n   165\t                revoke.setLong(1, now);\n   166\t                revoke.setLong(2, accountId);\n   167\t                int revoked = Integer.valueOf(revoke.executeUpdate());\n   168\t                if (revoked > 0) {\n   169\t                    gate.record(AuditLog.Event.SESSION_REVOKED, \"account\", gate.accountTag(accountId),\n   170\t                        \"scope\", \"all\", \"count\", Integer.toString(revoked));\n   171\t                }\n   172\t                return revoked;\n   173\t            }\n   174\t        });\n   175\t    }\n   176\t\n   177\t    /** Deletes expired or revoked rows, bounding the table. Returns the number removed. */\n   178\t    public int purgeExpired() throws SQLException, AuditLog.AuditException {\n   179\t        long now = clock.instant().getEpochSecond();\n   180\t        return db.inTransaction(connection -> {\n   181\t            try (PreparedStatement purge = connection.prepareStatement(\n   182\t                \"DELETE FROM sessions WHERE expires_at <= ? OR revoked_at IS NOT NULL\")) {\n   183\t                purge.setLong(1, now);\n   184\t                return Integer.valueOf(purge.executeUpdate());\n   185\t            }\n   186\t        });\n   187\t    }\n   188\t\n   189\t    /**\n   190\t     * Structural check only - 64 lower-case hex characters, the exact shape this class issues.\n   191\t     * It says nothing about validity, which only the lookup can decide.\n   192\t     */\n   193\t    public static boolean isWellFormedToken(String token) {\n   194\t        if (token == null || token.length() != TOKEN_CHARS) {\n   195\t            return false;\n   196\t        }\n   197\t        for (int i = 0; i < token.length(); i++) {\n   198\t            char c = token.charAt(i);\n   199\t            boolean digit = c >= '0' && c <= '9';\n   200\t            boolean letter = c >= 'a' && c <= 'f';\n   201\t            if (!digit && !letter) {\n   202\t                return false;\n   203\t            }\n   204\t        }\n   205\t        return true;\n   206\t    }\n   207\t\n   208\t    /** Count of live sessions, for the test suite's revocation assertions. */\n   209\t    public int liveSessionCount(long accountId) throws SQLException, AuditLog.AuditException {\n   210\t        long now = clock.instant().getEpochSecond();\n   211\t        return db.inTransaction(connection -> {\n   212\t            try (PreparedStatement count = connection.prepareStatement(\n   213\t                \"SELECT COUNT(*) FROM sessions WHERE account_id = ? AND revoked_at IS NULL AND expires_at > ?\")) {\n   214\t                count.setLong(1, accountId);\n   215\t                count.setLong(2, now);\n   216\t                try (ResultSet found = count.executeQuery()) {\n   217\t                    found.next();\n   218\t                    return Integer.valueOf(found.getInt(1));\n   219\t                }\n   220\t            }\n   221\t        });\n   222\t    }\n   223\t}\n===== Throttle.java =====\n     1\tpackage app;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.sql.Connection;\n     4\timport java.sql.PreparedStatement;\n     5\timport java.sql.ResultSet;\n     6\timport java.sql.SQLException;\n     7\timport java.time.Clock;\n     8\t\n     9\t/**\n    10\t * Rate limiting and temporary account lockout, enforced entirely in SQL.\n    11\t *\n    12\t * <p>Both controls are single atomic statements or single locked transactions, never a read\n    13\t * followed by a write in Java:\n    14\t * <ul>\n    15\t *   <li><b>Per source</b> - one {@code UPDATE ... WHERE <cond>} whose <em>row count</em> is the\n    16\t *       decision. A refill and a consume happen in the same statement, so N concurrent attempts\n    17\t *       cannot all observe the same token. Verified against H2 2.5.252: the statement reports\n    18\t *       zero affected rows once the bucket is empty.</li>\n    19\t *   <li><b>Per account</b> - the guard row is read {@code FOR UPDATE} inside the transaction, so\n    20\t *       two concurrent failures for one account serialise and the counter cannot be lost.</li>\n    21\t * </ul>\n    22\t *\n    23\t * <p>Lock ordering is always {@code throttle_bucket} then {@code accounts} then\n    24\t * {@code auth_guard}; following that order everywhere is what keeps two logins for different\n    25\t * accounts from deadlocking on each other. A lock wait exceeding\n    26\t * {@link Db#LOCK_TIMEOUT_MILLIS} raises {@code HYT00}, which callers translate into a refusal,\n    27\t * not a pass.\n    28\t *\n    29\t * <p>Lockout is temporary and self-healing by design: a permanent counter would let anyone who\n    30\t * knows an email address deny that person access. The counter also decays on its own\n    31\t * ({@code window_seconds}) and a successful sign-in clears it, so a legitimate user who waits\n    32\t * out the window is restored without operator action.\n    33\t */\n    34\tpublic final class Throttle {\n    35\t\n    36\t    /** Distinct key namespaces so one budget can never be drained by the other operation. */\n    37\t    public static final String LOGIN_ACCOUNT_SCOPE = \"login-account:\";\n    38\t    public static final String LOGIN_SOURCE_SCOPE = \"login-source:\";\n    39\t    public static final String REGISTER_SOURCE_SCOPE = \"register-source:\";\n    40\t\n    41\t    /** Failures tolerated per account inside its sliding window. */\n    42\t    static final int ACCOUNT_MAX_FAILURES = 5;\n    43\t    static final int ACCOUNT_WINDOW_SECONDS = 900;\n    44\t    /** How long an account stays locked once the budget is spent. */\n    45\t    static final int ACCOUNT_LOCK_SECONDS = 900;\n    46\t\n    47\t    /** Attempts allowed per source before refusals start, and the window they are measured in. */\n    48\t    static final int SOURCE_MAX_ATTEMPTS = 10;\n    49\t    static final int SOURCE_WINDOW_SECONDS = 300;\n    50\t\n    51\t    private final Db db;\n    52\t    private final Clock clock;\n    53\t\n    54\t    public Throttle(Db db, Clock clock) {\n    55\t        this.db = db;\n    56\t        this.clock = clock;\n    57\t    }\n    58\t\n    59\t    private long now() {\n    60\t        return clock.instant().getEpochSecond();\n    61\t    }\n    62\t\n    63\t    /**\n    64\t     * Consumes one attempt from a source's budget. Must be called inside the caller's\n    65\t     * transaction so the consume and the work it protects commit together.\n    66\t     *\n    67\t     * @return {@code true} when the attempt is allowed; {@code false} when the budget is spent\n    68\t     */\n    69\t    public boolean tryConsumeSource(Connection connection, String scope, String sourceId) throws SQLException {\n    70\t        if (sourceId == null || sourceId.isEmpty() || sourceId.length() > 128) {\n    71\t            // A caller that cannot name the source cannot be rate-limited by source; refusing is\n    72\t            // the fail-closed choice, and every in-tree caller supplies a hashed scope key.\n    73\t            return false;\n    74\t        }\n    75\t        String key = scope + sourceId;\n    76\t        long now = now();\n    77\t        insertBucketIfAbsent(connection, key, now);\n    78\t        int taken;\n    79\t        try (PreparedStatement consume = connection.prepareStatement(\"\"\"\n    80\t            UPDATE throttle_bucket\n    81\t               SET tokens = CASE WHEN (? - window_start) >= window_seconds\n    82\t                                 THEN max_tokens - 1 ELSE tokens - 1 END,\n    83\t                   window_start = CASE WHEN (? - window_start) >= window_seconds\n    84\t                                       THEN ? ELSE window_start END\n    85\t             WHERE bucket_key = ?\n    86\t               AND (CASE WHEN (? - window_start) >= window_seconds THEN max_tokens ELSE tokens END) > 0\n    87\t            \"\"\")) {\n    88\t            consume.setLong(1, now);\n    89\t            consume.setLong(2, now);\n    90\t            consume.setLong(3, now);\n    91\t            consume.setString(4, key);\n    92\t            consume.setLong(5, now);\n    93\t            taken = consume.executeUpdate();\n    94\t        }\n    95\t        // Zero affected rows means the budget is spent: deny without touching the row.\n    96\t        return taken == 1;\n    97\t    }\n    98\t\n    99\t    private void insertBucketIfAbsent(Connection connection, String key, long now) throws SQLException {\n   100\t        try (PreparedStatement create = connection.prepareStatement(\"\"\"\n   101\t            INSERT INTO throttle_bucket (bucket_key, tokens, window_start, window_seconds, max_tokens)\n   102\t            VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?)\n   103\t            \"\"\")) {\n   104\t            create.setString(1, key);\n   105\t            create.setInt(2, SOURCE_MAX_ATTEMPTS);\n   106\t            create.setLong(3, now);\n   107\t            create.setInt(4, SOURCE_WINDOW_SECONDS);\n   108\t            create.setInt(5, SOURCE_MAX_ATTEMPTS);\n   109\t            create.executeUpdate();\n   110\t        } catch (SQLException alreadyExists) {\n   111\t            if (!isUniqueViolation(alreadyExists)) {\n   112\t                throw alreadyExists;\n   113\t            }\n   114\t        }\n   115\t    }\n   116\t\n   117\t    /**\n   118\t     * Reads the guard row for an account, creating it when absent, under a row lock so the\n   119\t     * caller's subsequent failure/success update cannot interleave with another transaction.\n   120\t     *\n   121\t     * <p>Two concurrent first-time attempts for the same account can both see no row and race to\n   122\t     * insert. The loser catches the unique violation and re-reads under the lock, which is safe\n   123\t     * because H2 2.5.252 keeps the transaction usable after a caught constraint violation\n   124\t     * (verified: a subsequent statement in the same transaction still reports its affected rows).\n   125\t     */\n   126\t    public AccountGuard lockAccountGuard(Connection connection, long accountId) throws SQLException {\n   127\t        AccountGuard existing = readGuardForUpdate(connection, accountId);\n   128\t        if (existing != null) {\n   129\t            return existing;\n   130\t        }\n   131\t        try (PreparedStatement create = connection.prepareStatement(\n   132\t            \"INSERT INTO auth_guard (account_id, failures, window_start, window_seconds, max_failures, blocked_until, version)\"\n   133\t                + \" VALUES (?, 0, ?, ?, ?, 0, 0)\")) {\n   134\t            create.setLong(1, accountId);\n   135\t            create.setLong(2, now());\n   136\t            create.setInt(3, ACCOUNT_WINDOW_SECONDS);\n   137\t            create.setInt(4, ACCOUNT_MAX_FAILURES);\n   138\t            create.executeUpdate();\n   139\t        } catch (SQLException alreadyExists) {\n   140\t            if (!isUniqueViolation(alreadyExists)) {\n   141\t                throw alreadyExists;\n   142\t            }\n   143\t        }\n   144\t        AccountGuard created = readGuardForUpdate(connection, accountId);\n   145\t        if (created == null) {\n   146\t            throw new SQLException(\"the authentication guard row could not be established\");\n   147\t        }\n   148\t        return created;\n   149\t    }\n   150\t\n   151\t    private AccountGuard readGuardForUpdate(Connection connection, long accountId) throws SQLException {\n   152\t        try (PreparedStatement read = connection.prepareStatement(\n   153\t            \"SELECT failures, window_start, blocked_until FROM auth_guard WHERE account_id = ? FOR UPDATE\")) {\n   154\t            read.setLong(1, accountId);\n   155\t            try (ResultSet found = read.executeQuery()) {\n   156\t                return found.next() ? new AccountGuard(found.getInt(1), found.getLong(2), found.getLong(3)) : null;\n   157\t            }\n   158\t        }\n   159\t    }\n   160\t\n   161\t    /**\n   162\t     * Charges one failure against an account. Locks once the budget is spent. The caller still\n   163\t     * reports the generic invalid-credential result for this attempt; {@code LOCKED} is only\n   164\t     * reported when a later attempt is refused without checking the password at all.\n   165\t     *\n   166\t     * @return the remaining lock state, for logging\n   167\t     */\n   168\t    public long chargeFailure(Connection connection, long accountId, AccountGuard before) throws SQLException {\n   169\t        long now = now();\n   170\t        int failures = before.windowExpired(now) ? 1 : before.failures() + 1;\n   171\t        long windowStart = before.windowExpired(now) ? now : before.windowStart();\n   172\t        long blockedUntil = failures >= ACCOUNT_MAX_FAILURES ? now + ACCOUNT_LOCK_SECONDS : 0;\n   173\t        try (PreparedStatement write = connection.prepareStatement(\"\"\"\n   174\t            UPDATE auth_guard\n   175\t               SET failures = ?, window_start = ?, blocked_until = ?, version = version + 1\n   176\t             WHERE account_id = ?\n   177\t            \"\"\")) {\n   178\t            write.setInt(1, failures);\n   179\t            write.setLong(2, windowStart);\n   180\t            write.setLong(3, blockedUntil);\n   181\t            write.setLong(4, accountId);\n   182\t            int written = write.executeUpdate();\n   183\t            if (written != 1) {\n   184\t                throw new SQLException(\"the authentication guard row disappeared mid-transaction\");\n   185\t            }\n   186\t        }\n   187\t        return blockedUntil;\n   188\t    }\n   189\t\n   190\t    /** Clears the failure counter and any lock: a proven sign-in resets the account's history. */\n   191\t    public void clearFailures(Connection connection, long accountId) throws SQLException {\n   192\t        try (PreparedStatement clear = connection.prepareStatement(\n   193\t            \"UPDATE auth_guard SET failures = 0, window_start = ?, blocked_until = 0, version = version + 1\"\n   194\t                + \" WHERE account_id = ?\")) {\n   195\t            clear.setLong(1, now());\n   196\t            clear.setLong(2, accountId);\n   197\t            clear.executeUpdate();\n   198\t        }\n   199\t    }\n   200\t\n   201\t    /**\n   202\t     * Deletes throttle state that can no longer affect a decision, so the two tables cannot grow\n   203\t     * without bound as a program The unknown-account and locked-account paths derive\n    16\t * against {@link #dummyHash()}: the same memory-hard work against a constant credential that can\n    17\t * never match, so neither wall-clock time nor peak memory reveals which branch ran. There is no\n    18\t * sleep-based pad, so the padding cannot drift out of step with the configured cost. A successful\n    19\t * sign-in on an account whose stored parameters are below policy does one extra derivation to\n    20\t * upgrade it - a path that already required the correct password.\n    21\t *\n    22\t * <h2>Atomicity</h2>\n    23\t * One transaction per attempt, in a fixed order: consume the source budget, read the account, take\n    24\t * the {@code auth_guard} row lock, verify, then either clear or charge the account counter and\n    25\t * insert the session. Any failure rolls the whole attempt back, so a session cannot exist without\n    26\t * its account, and a failure cannot be charged to an account without the source budget also being\n    27\t * spent.\n    28\t *\n    29\t * <h2>Lockout is not a denial-of-access lever</h2>\n    30\t * The per-account counter is temporary ({@link Throttle#ACCOUNT_LOCK_SECONDS}) and self-clearing,\n    31\t * and a successful sign-in clears it, so knowing a colleague's email does not let an attacker keep\n    32\t * them out beyond one window. Total attempt rate is bounded by the per-source budget, which is\n    33\t * independent of the account: burning an account's five strikes costs the attacker five of their\n    34\t * own ten.\n    35\t */\n    36\tpublic final class AuthService {\n    37\t\n    38\t    /** Single sign-in refusal for every failure mode, including a locked account. */\n    39\t    public static final String INVALID_MESSAGE = \"The email or password is not correct.\";\n    40\t\n    41\t    /** Shown when the audit trail or the store cannot be honoured: access is refused. */\n    42\t    public static final String INTERNAL_MESSAGE = \"Sign-in is unavailable right now. The unknown-account and locked-account paths derive\n    16\t * against {@link #dummyHash()}: the same memory-hard work against a constant credential that can\n    17\t * never match, so neither wall-clock time nor peak memory reveals which branch ran. There is no\n    18\t * sleep-based pad, so the padding cannot drift out of step with the configured cost. A successful\n    19\t * sign-in on an account whose stored parameters are below policy does one extra derivation to\n    20\t * upgrade it - a path that already required the correct password.\n    21\t *\n    22\t * <h2>Atomicity</h2>\n    23\t * One transaction per attempt, in a fixed order: consume the source budget, read the account, take\n    24\t * the {@code auth_guard} row lock, verify, then either clear or charge the account counter and\n    25\t * insert the session. Any failure rolls the whole attempt back, so a session cannot exist without\n    26\t * its account, and a failure cannot be charged to an account without the source budget also being\n    27\t * spent.\n    28\t *\n    29\t * <h2>Lockout is not a denial-of-access lever</h2>\n    30\t * The per-account counter is temporary ({@link Throttle#ACCOUNT_LOCK_SECONDS}) and self-clearing,\n    31\t * and a successful sign-in clears it, so knowing a colleague's email does not let an attacker keep\n    32\t * them out beyond one window. {"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_16","type":"command_execution","command":"/bin/bash -lc 'nl -ba /data/results/qwen/set_a/post_research/4/security/src/main/java/app/Maintenance.java'","aggregated_output":"     1\tpackage app;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.sql.SQLException;\n     4\t\n     5\t/**\n     6\t * Bounds the state that the feature itself creates.\n     7\t *\n     8\t * <p>Every failed sign-in, every distinct source and every issued session writes a row. Without a\n     9\t * sweep the store only grows, and unbounded growth in a security table is its own problem: it\n    10\t * slows the very lookups that decide access and eventually exhausts the disk, which the audit\n    11\t * trail turns into a denial. So the state that can no longer affect a decision is deleted -\n    12\t * expired or revoked sessions, elapsed throttle windows, and guard rows for accounts that are both\n    13\t * outside the recent window <em>and</em> hold no live state.\n    14\t *\n    15\t * <p>A sweep never weakens a control that is still in force. A source bucket is eligible only once\n    16\t * its whole window has elapsed, which is the moment its next attempt would refill it anyway, so\n    17\t * deleting rows cannot be used to reset a denial that is currently blocking somebody. A guard row is\n    18\t * kept for the same reason even when its account has aged out of retention: if its lockout has not\n    19\t * expired, or its failure window is still running, deleting it would hand a rate limit or a lockout\n    20\t * back to whoever triggered the sweep.\n    21\t *\n    22\t * <p>Failure to sweep is reported and ignored, never fatal and never a denial: this is a\n    23\t * housekeeping task, and refusing a sign-in because old rows were not deleted would be a denial of\n    24\t * service caused by maintenance rather than by an attack.\n    25\t */\n    26\tpublic final class Maintenance {\n    27\t\n    28\t    /** Guard rows are kept for this many of the most recently created accounts. */\n    29\t    static final int RECENT_ACCOUNTS_TO_KEEP = 10_000;\n    30\t\n    31\t    private final SessionStore sessions;\n    32\t    private final Throttle throttle;\n    33\t    private final Gate gate;\n    34\t\n    35\t    public Maintenance(SessionStore sessions, Throttle throttle, Gate gate) {\n    36\t        this.sessions = sessions;\n    37\t        this.throttle = throttle;\n    38\t        this.gate = gate;\n    39\t    }\n    40\t\n    41\t    /** Runs one sweep; returns the number of rows removed, which is zero when the sweep failed. */\n    42\t    public int run() {\n    43\t        int sessionsRemoved;\n    44\t        int throttleRemoved;\n    45\t        try {\n    46\t            sessionsRemoved = sessions.purgeExpired();\n    47\t            throttleRemoved = throttle.purgeStaleState(RECENT_ACCOUNTS_TO_KEEP);\n    48\t        } catch (SQLException | AuditLog.AuditException sweepFailed) {\n    49\t            System.err.println(\"NOTE the maintenance sweep could not complete;\"\n    50\t                + \" the operation that triggered it is unaffected\");\n    51\t            return 0;\n    52\t        }\n    53\t        int removed = sessionsRemoved + throttleRemoved;\n    54\t        // Only a sweep that changed state is recorded. This runs on every invocation, so a record per\n    55\t        // no-op would make the trail grow in proportion to how often the program is started - the one\n    56\t        // resource the audit budget exists to bound.\n    57\t        if (removed == 0) {\n    58\t            return 0;\n    59\t        }\n    60\t        try {\n    61\t            gate.record(AuditLog.Event.MAINTENANCE_RUN,\n    62\t                \"sessions\", Integer.toString(sessionsRemoved),\n    63\t                \"throttle\", Integer.toString(throttleRemoved));\n    64\t        } catch (AuditLog.AuditException auditUnwritable) {\n    65\t            // Nothing was denied by this record, so an unwritable trail must not undo a sweep that\n    66\t            // already committed. Every method throws {@link AuditLog.AuditException} on a write failure, and callers\n     6\t * translate that into a refusal - the trail cannot be silently lost by an attacker who fills the\n     7\t * disk, replaces the log with a directory, or widens its permissions.\n     8\t *\n     9\t * <p>Field values passed through here are always code-supplied constants or non-reversible\n    10\t * digests; see {@link AuditLog}.\n    11\t */\n    12\tpublic final class Gate {\n    13\t\n    14\t    /** Per-process salt so correlation tags do not survive as stable row identifiers. */\n    15\t    private final String processSalt = java.util.UUID.randomUUID().toString();\n    16\t\n    17\t    private final AuditLog audit;\n    18\t\n    19\t    public Gate(AuditLog audit) {\n    20\t        this.audit = audit;\n    21\t    }\n    22\t\n    23\t    /** Records the event or throws; there is no variant that swallows a failure. */\n    24\t    public void record(AuditLog.Event event, String... fields) throws AuditLog.AuditException {\n    25\t        audit.record(event, fields);\n    26\t    }\n    27\t\n    28\t    /**\n    29\t     * Non-reversible correlation tag for an account: the primary key hashed with a per-process\n    30\t     * random salt, so records can be joined without writing a row identifier that maps directly\n    31\t     * onto the database.\n    32\t     */\n    33\t    public String accountTag(long accountId) {\n    34\t        return tag(\"account\", Long.toString(accountId));\n    35\t    }\n    36\t\n    37\t    /** Non-reversible tag for a source identifier. */\n    38\t    public String sourceTag(String sourceId) {\n    39\t        if (sourceId == null) {\n    40\t            return \"unknown\";\n    41\t        }\n    42\t        return tag(\"src\", sourceId);\n    43\t    }\n    44\t\n    45\t    /**\n    46\t     * Tags for identifying data. See the\n    49\t     * README for exactly what that does and does not protect.\n    50\t     */\n    51\t    public String emailTag(String canonicalEmail) {\n    52\t        return tag(\"email\", canonicalEmail);\n    53\t    }\n    54\t\n    55\t    public String usernameTag(String username) {\n    56\t        return tag(\"username\", username);\n    57\t    }\n    58\t\n    59\t    /**\n    60\t     * Tag for a session token. {"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_23","type":"command_execution","command":"/bin/bash -lc 'nl -ba /data/results/qwen/set_a/post_research/4/security/src/main/java/app/Maintenance.java'","aggregated_output":"     1\tpackage app;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.sql.SQLException;\n     4\t\n     5\t/**\n     6\t * Bounds the state that the feature itself creates.\n     7\t *\n     8\t * <p>Every failed sign-in, every distinct source and every issued session writes a row. Without a\n     9\t * sweep the store only grows, and unbounded growth in a security table is its own problem: it\n    10\t * slows the very lookups that decide access and eventually exhausts the disk, which the audit\n    11\t * trail turns into a denial. So the state that can no longer affect a decision is deleted -\n    12\t * expired or revoked sessions, elapsed throttle windows, and guard rows for accounts that are both\n    13\t * outside the recent window <em>and</em> hold no live state.\n    14\t *\n    15\t * <p>A sweep never weakens a control that is still in force. A source bucket is eligible only once\n    16\t * its whole window has elapsed, which is the moment its next attempt would refill it anyway, so\n    17\t * deleting rows cannot be used to reset a denial that is currently blocking somebody. A guard row is\n    18\t * kept for the same reason even when its account has aged out of retention: if its lockout has not\n    19\t * expired, or its failure window is still running, deleting it would hand a rate limit or a lockout\n    20\t * back to whoever triggered the sweep.\n    21\t *\n    22\t * <p>Failure to sweep is reported and ignored, never fatal and never a denial: this is a\n    23\t * housekeeping task, and refusing a sign-in because old rows were not deleted would be a denial of\n    24\t * service caused by maintenance rather than by an attack.\n    25\t */\n    26\tpublic final class Maintenance {\n    27\t\n    28\t    /** Guard rows are kept for this many of the most recently created accounts. */\n    29\t    static final int RECENT_ACCOUNTS_TO_KEEP = 10_000;\n    30\t\n    31\t    private final SessionStore sessions;\n    32\t    private final Throttle throttle;\n    33\t    private final Gate gate;\n    34\t\n    35\t    public Maintenance(SessionStore sessions, Throttle throttle, Gate gate) {\n    36\t        this.sessions = sessions;\n    37\t        this.throttle = throttle;\n    38\t        this.gate = gate;\n    39\t    }\n    40\t\n    41\t    /** Runs one sweep; This runs on every invocation, so a record per\n    55\t        // no-op would make the trail grow in proportion to how often the program is started - the one\n    56\t        // resource the audit budget exists to bound.\n    57\t        if (removed == 0) {\n    58\t            return 0;\n    59\t        }\n    60\t        try {\n    61\t            gate.record(AuditLog.Event.MAINTENANCE_RUN,\n    62\t                \"sessions\", Integer.toString(sessionsRemoved),\n    63\t                \"throttle\", Integer.toString(throttleRemoved));\n    64\t        } catch (AuditLog.AuditException auditUnwritable) {\n    65\t            // Nothing was denied by this record, so an unwritable trail must not undo a sweep that\n    66\t            // already committed.
