Combine with authentication, authorization, and WAF rules.\n\n## Prerequisites\n\n- Redis 6.0+ for distributed rate limit counters (or in-memory for single-instance deployments)\n- API framework (Express.js, FastAPI, Spring Boot, or Django REST Framework)\n- Monitoring system for rate limit metrics (Prometheus, CloudWatch, Datadog)\n- Understanding of the API's normal traffic patterns and peak usage\n- Load testing tool (k6, Gatling, or Locust) for validating rate limit behavior\n\n## Workflow\n\n### Step 1: Rate Limiting Strategy Design\n\nDefine rate limits per endpoint category and user tier:\n\n```python\n# Rate limit configuration\nRATE_LIMITS = {\n    # Authentication endpoints (most restrictive)\n    \"auth\": {\n        \"login\": {\"requests\": 5, \"window_seconds\": 60, \"by\": \"ip\"},\n        \"register\": {\"requests\": 3, \"window_seconds\": 300, \"by\": \"ip\"},\n        \"forgot_password\": {\"requests\": 3, \"window_seconds\": 3600, \"by\": \"ip\"},\n        \"verify_mfa\": {\"requests\": 5, \"window_seconds\": 300, \"by\": \"user\"},\n    },\n    # Standard API endpoints\n    \"api\": {\n        \"free\": {\"requests\": 60, \"window_seconds\": 60, \"by\": \"user\"},\n        \"premium\": {\"requests\": 300, \"window_seconds\": 60, \"by\": \"user\"},\n        \"enterprise\": {\"requests\": 1000, \"window_seconds\": 60, \"by\": \"user\"},\n    },\n    # Resource-intensive endpoints\n    \"expensive\": {\n        \"search\": {\"requests\": 10, \"window_seconds\": 60, \"by\": \"user\"},\n        \"export\": {\"requests\": 5, \"window_seconds\": 3600, \"by\": \"user\"},\n        \"bulk_import\": {\"requests\": 2, \"window_seconds\": 3600, \"by\": \"user\"},\n    },\n    # Global limits\n    \"global\": {\n        \"per_ip\": {\"requests\": 1000, \"window_seconds\": 60, \"by\": \"ip\"},\n        \"per_user\": {\"requests\": 5000, \"window_seconds\": 3600, \"by\": \"user\"},\n    },\n}\n```\n\n### Step 2: Sliding Window Rate Limiter (Redis)\n\n```python\nimport redis\nimport time\nimport hashlib\nfrom functools import wraps\nfrom flask import Flask, request, jsonify, g\n\napp = Flask(__name__)\nredis_client = redis.Redis(host='localhost', port=6379, db=0, decode_responses=True)\n\nclass SlidingWindowRateLimiter:\n    \"\"\"Sliding window rate limiter using Redis sorted sets.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, redis_conn):\n        self.redis = redis_conn\n\n    def is_allowed(self, key, max_requests, window_seconds):\n        \"\"\"Check if request is allowed and record it.\"\"\"\n        now = time.time()\n        window_start = now - window_seconds\n        pipe = self.redis.pipeline()\n\n        # Remove expired entries\n        pipe.zremrangebyscore(key, 0, window_start)\n        # Count requests in current window\n        pipe.zcard(key)\n        # Add current request\n        pipe.zadd(key, {f\"{now}:{hashlib.md5(str(now).encode()).hexdigest()[:8]}\": now})\n        # Set TTL on the key\n        pipe.expire(key, window_seconds + 1)\n\n        results = pipe.execute()\n        current_count = results[1]\n\n        if current_count >= max_requests:\n            # Calculate retry-after\n            oldest = self.redis.zrange(key, 0, 0, withscores=True)\n            if oldest:\n                retry_after = int(oldest[0][1] + window_seconds - now) + 1\n            else:\n                retry_after = window_seconds\n            return False, current_count, max_requests, retry_after\n\n        return True, current_count + 1, max_requests, 0\n\nrate_limiter = SlidingWindowRateLimiter(redis_client)\n\ndef rate_limit(max_requests, window_seconds, key_func=None):\n    \"\"\"Decorator for rate limiting API endpoints.\"\"\"\n    def decorator(f):\n        @wraps(f)\n        def wrapped(*args, **kwargs):\n            # Determine the rate limit key\n            if key_func:\n                identifier = key_func()\n            elif hasattr(g, 'user_id'):\n                identifier = f\"user:{g.user_id}\"\n            else:\n                identifier = f\"ip:{request.remote_addr}\"\n\n            key = f\"ratelimit:{request.endpoint}:{identifier}\"\n            allowed, current, limit, retry_after = rate_limiter.is_allowed(\n                key, max_requests, window_seconds)\n\n            # Always set rate limit headers\n            headers = {\n                \"X-RateLimit-Limit\": str(limit),\n                \"X-RateLimit-Remaining\": str(max(0, limit - current)),\n                \"X-RateLimit-Reset\": str(int(time.time()) + window_seconds),\n            }\n\n            if not allowed:\n                headers[\"Retry-After\"] = str(retry_after)\n                response = jsonify({\n                    \"error\": \"rate_limit_exceeded\",\n                    \"message\": \"Too many requests. Please try again later.\",\n                    \"retry_after\": retry_after\n                })\n                response.status_code = 429\n                for h, v in headers.items():\n                    response.headers[h] = v\n                return response\n\n            response = f(*args, **kwargs)\n            for h, v in headers.items():\n                response.headers[h] = v\n            return response\n        return wrapped\n    return decorator\n\n# Apply rate limiting to endpoints\n@app.route('/api/v1/auth/login', methods=['POST'])\n@rate_limit(max_requests=5, window_seconds=60,\n            key_func=lambda: f\"ip:{request.remote_addr}\")\ndef login():\n    # Login logic\n    return jsonify({\"message\": \"Login successful\"})\n\n@app.route('/api/v1/users/me', methods=['GET'])\n@rate_limit(max_requests=60, window_seconds=60)\ndef get_profile():\n    # Profile logic\n    return jsonify({\"user\": \"data\"})\n\n@app.route('/api/v1/search', methods=['GET'])\n@rate_limit(max_requests=10, window_seconds=60)\ndef search():\n    # Search logic\n    return jsonify({\"results\": []})\n```\n\n### Step 3: Token Bucket Rate Limiter\n\n```python\nimport redis\nimport time\n\nclass TokenBucketRateLimiter:\n    \"\"\"Token bucket rate limiter allowing burst traffic within limits.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, redis_conn):\n        self.redis = redis_conn\n\n    def is_allowed(self, key, max_tokens, refill_rate, refill_interval=1):\n        \"\"\"\n        Token bucket algorithm:\n        - max_tokens: Maximum burst capacity\n        - refill_rate: Tokens added per refill_interval\n        - refill_interval: Seconds between refills\n        \"\"\"\n        now = time.time()\n        bucket_key = f\"tb:{key}\"\n\n        # Lua script for atomic token bucket operation\n        lua_script = \"\"\"\n        local key = KEYS[1]\n        local max_tokens = tonumber(ARGV[1])\n        local refill_rate = tonumber(ARGV[2])\n        local refill_interval = tonumber(ARGV[3])\n        local now = tonumber(ARGV[4])\n\n        local bucket = redis.call('hmget', key, 'tokens', 'last_refill')\n        local tokens = tonumber(bucket[1])\n        local last_refill = tonumber(bucket[2])\n\n        if tokens == nil then\n            tokens = max_tokens\n            last_refill = now\n        end\n\n        -- Refill tokens\n        local elapsed = now - last_refill\n        local refills = math.floor(elapsed / refill_interval)\n        if refills > 0 then\n            tokens = math.min(max_tokens, tokens + (refills * refill_rate))\n            last_refill = last_refill + (refills * refill_interval)\n        end\n\n        local allowed = 0\n        if tokens >= 1 then\n            tokens = tokens - 1\n            allowed = 1\n        end\n\n        redis.call('hmset', key, 'tokens', tokens, 'last_refill', last_refill)\n        redis.call('expire', key, math.ceil(max_tokens / refill_rate * refill_interval) + 10)\n\n        return {allowed, tokens, max_tokens}\n        \"\"\"\n\n        result = self.redis.eval(lua_script, 1, bucket_key,\n                                  max_tokens, refill_rate, refill_interval, now)\n        allowed = bool(result[0])\n        remaining = int(result[1])\n        limit = int(result[2])\n\n        return allowed, remaining, limit\n```\n\n### Step 4: Tiered Rate Limiting with User Plans\n\n```python\nfrom enum import Enum\n\nclass UserTier(Enum):\n    FREE = \"free\"\n    PREMIUM = \"premium\"\n    ENTERPRISE = \"enterprise\"\n\nTIER_LIMITS = {\n    UserTier.FREE: {\n        \"default\": (60, 60),          # 60 req/min\n        \"search\": (10, 60),           # 10 req/min\n        \"export\": (5, 3600),          # 5 req/hour\n        \"daily_total\": (1000, 86400), # 1000 req/day\n    },\n    UserTier.PREMIUM: {\n        \"default\": (300, 60),\n        \"search\": (50, 60),\n        \"export\": (20, 3600),\n        \"daily_total\": (10000, 86400),\n    },\n    UserTier.ENTERPRISE: {\n        \"default\": (1000, 60),\n        \"search\": (200, 60),\n        \"export\": (100, 3600),\n        \"daily_total\": (100000, 86400),\n    },\n}\n\ndef get_rate_limit_for_request(user_tier, endpoint_category=\"default\"):\n    \"\"\"Get rate limit configuration based on user tier and endpoint.\"\"\"\n    tier_config = TIER_LIMITS.get(user_tier, TIER_LIMITS[UserTier.FREE])\n    limit_config = tier_config.get(endpoint_category, tier_config[\"default\"])\n    return limit_config  # (max_requests, window_seconds)\n\nclass TieredRateLimitMiddleware:\n    \"\"\"Middleware that applies rate limits based on user subscription tier.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, app, redis_conn):\n        self.app = app\n        self.limiter = SlidingWindowRateLimiter(redis_conn)\n\n    def __call__(self, environ, start_response):\n        # Extract user info from request\n        user_id = environ.get(\"HTTP_X_USER_ID\")\n        user_tier = UserTier(environ.get(\"HTTP_X_USER_TIER\", \"free\"))\n        endpoint = environ.get(\"PATH_INFO\", \"/\")\n\n        # Determine endpoint category\n        category = \"default\"\n        if \"/search\" in endpoint:\n            category = \"search\"\n        elif \"/export\" in endpoint:\n            category = \"export\"\n\n        max_requests, window = get_rate_limit_for_request(user_tier, category)\n        key = f\"tiered:{user_id or environ.get('REMOTE_ADDR')}:{category}\"\n\n        allowed, current, limit, retry_after = self.limiter.is_allowed(\n            key, max_requests, window)\n\n        if not allowed:\n            status = \"429 Too Many Requests\"\n            headers = [\n                (\"Content-Type\", \"application/json\"),\n                (\"Retry-After\", str(retry_after)),\n                (\"X-RateLimit-Limit\", str(limit)),\n                (\"X-RateLimit-Remaining\", \"0\"),\n            ]\n            start_response(status, headers)\n            body = f'{{\"error\":\"rate_limit_exceeded\",\"retry_after\":{retry_after},\"tier\":\"{user_tier.value}\"}}'\n            return [body.encode()]\n\n        return self.app(environ, start_response)\n```\n\n### Step 5: Distributed Rate Limiting for Microservices\n\n```python\n# Centralized rate limiting service using Redis Cluster\nimport redis\nfrom redis.cluster import RedisCluster\n\nclass DistributedRateLimiter:\n    \"\"\"Rate limiter for microservice architectures using Redis Cluster.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self):\n        self.redis = RedisCluster(\n            startup_nodes=[\n                {\"host\": \"redis-node-1\", \"port\": 6379},\n                {\"host\": \"redis-node-2\", \"port\": 6379},\n                {\"host\": \"redis-node-3\", \"port\": 6379},\n            ],\n            decode_responses=True\n        )\n\n    def check_and_increment(self, service_name, user_id, endpoint,\n                             max_requests, window_seconds):\n        \"\"\"Atomic check-and-increment using Redis Lua script.\"\"\"\n        key = f\"rl:{{{service_name}}}:{user_id}:{endpoint}\"\n\n        # Lua script ensures atomicity across the check and increment\n        lua_script = \"\"\"\n        local key = KEYS[1]\n        local max_requests = tonumber(ARGV[1])\n        local window = tonumber(ARGV[2])\n        local now = tonumber(ARGV[3])\n        local window_start = now - window\n\n        -- Remove old entries\n        redis.call('zremrangebyscore', key, '-inf', window_start)\n\n        -- Count current entries\n        local count = redis.call('zcard', key)\n\n        if count >= max_requests then\n            -- Get oldest entry for retry-after calculation\n            local oldest = redis.call('zrange', key, 0, 0, 'WITHSCORES')\n            local retry_after = 0\n            if #oldest > 0 then\n                retry_after = math.ceil(tonumber(oldest[2]) + window - now)\n            end\n            return {0, count, retry_after}\n        end\n\n        -- Add new entry\n        redis.call('zadd', key, now, now .. ':' .. math.random(100000))\n        redis.call('expire', key, window + 1)\n\n        return {1, count + 1, 0}\n        \"\"\"\n\n        result = self.redis.eval(lua_script, 1, key,\n                                  max_requests, window_seconds, time.time())\n        return {\n            \"allowed\": bool(result[0]),\n            \"current\": int(result[1]),\n            \"retry_after\": int(result[2]),\n        }\n```\n\n## Key Concepts\n\n| Term | Definition |\n|------|------------|\n| **Sliding Window** | Rate limiting algorithm that tracks requests in a rolling time window, providing smoother rate enforcement than fixed windows |\n| **Token Bucket** | Algorithm where tokens are added at a fixed rate and consumed per request, allowing controlled bursts up to the bucket capacity |\n| **Fixed Window** | Simplest rate limiting where requests are counted per fixed time window (e.g., per minute), susceptible to burst at window boundaries |\n| **429 Too Many Requests** | HTTP status code indicating the client has exceeded the rate limit, accompanied by Retry-After header |\n| **Retry-After Header** | HTTP response header telling the client how many seconds to wait before retrying, essential for well-behaved API clients |\n| **Distributed Rate Limiting** | Rate limiting across multiple server instances using shared state (Redis, Memcached) to maintain accurate global counters |\n\n## Tools & Systems\n\n- **Redis**: In-memory data store used for distributed rate limit counters with atomic operations via Lua scripts\n- **Kong Rate Limiting Plugin**: API gateway plugin supporting fixed-window and sliding-window rate limiting with Redis backend\n- **express-rate-limit**: Express.js middleware for simple rate limiting with Redis, Memcached, or in-memory stores\n- **Flask-Limiter**: Flask extension for rate limiting with support for multiple backends and configurable limits per endpoint\n- **Envoy Rate Limit Service**: Centralized rate limiting service for Envoy-based service mesh architectures\n\n## Common Scenarios\n\n### Scenario: Implementing Rate Limiting for a Public API\n\n**Context**: A company launches a public API with free, premium, and enterprise tiers. The API must protect against abuse while providing fair access to paying customers. The API runs on 6 instances behind an AWS ALB.\n\n**Approach**:\n1. Deploy Redis Cluster (3 nodes) for distributed rate limit state\n2. Implement sliding window rate limiter using Redis sorted sets with Lua scripts for atomicity\n3. Configure per-tier limits: Free (60 req/min), Premium (300 req/min), Enterprise (1000 req/min)\n4. Add stricter limits on authentication endpoints (5 req/min per IP) regardless of tier\n5. Implement resource-intensive endpoint limits (search: 10 req/min free, export: 5 req/hour)\n6. Set rate limit response headers on every response (X-RateLimit-Limit, X-RateLimit-Remaining, X-RateLimit-Reset)\n7. Return 429 with Retry-After header and JSON error body when limits are exceeded\n8. Set up Prometheus metrics for rate limit hits and CloudWatch alarms for unusual patterns\n\n**Pitfalls**:\n- Using in-memory rate limiting without shared state across instances, allowing limit bypass by hitting different servers\n- Not implementing rate limiting on authentication endpoints separately from general API limits\n- Using fixed windows that allow burst at window boundaries (2x the limit in a short period)\n- Not including rate limit headers on successful responses, giving clients no visibility into their quota\n- Trusting X-Forwarded-For for IP identification without validating it against the load balancer\n\n## Output Format\n\n```\n## Rate Limiting Implementation Report\n\n**API**: Public API v2\n**Algorithm**: Sliding Window (Redis Sorted Sets)\n**Backend**: Redis Cluster (3 nodes)\n**Deployment**: 6 API instances behind AWS ALB\n\n### Rate Limit Configuration\n\n| Tier | Default | Search | Export | Auth (per IP) |\n|------|---------|--------|--------|---------------|\n| Free | 60/min | 10/min | 5/hour | 5/min |\n| Premium | 300/min | 50/min | 20/hour | 5/min |\n| Enterprise | 1000/min | 200/min | 100/hour | 10/min |\n\n### Validation Results (k6 load test)\n\n- Free tier: Rate limited at 61st request (correct)\n- Premium tier: Rate limited at 301st request (correct)\n- Cross-instance: Rate limiting consistent across all 6 instances\n- Redis failover: Rate limiting degrades gracefully (allows traffic) when Redis is unreachable\n- Retry-After header: Accurate within 1 second of actual reset time\n- Response overhead: < 2ms added latency per request for rate limit check\n```\n/root/.codex/skills/implementing-api-rate-limiting-and-throttling/references/api-reference.md\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

self.last_refill) * self.refill_rate)\n        self.last_refill = now\n        if self.tokens >= 1:\n            self.tokens -= 1\n            return True\n        return False\n```\n\n## Redis Sliding Window\n\n```python\nimport redis, time\nr = redis.Redis()\ndef check_rate(client_id, window=60, limit=100):\n    key = f\"rl:{client_id}\"\n    now = time.time()\n    pipe = r.pipeline()\n    pipe.zremrangebyscore(key, 0, now - window)\n    pipe.zadd(key, {str(now): now})\n    pipe.zcard(key)\n    pipe.expire(key, window)\n    _, _, count, _ = pipe.execute()\n    return count <= limit\n```\n\n## HTTP 429 Response Headers\n\n| Header | Value | Description |\n|--------|-------|-------------|\n| `Retry-After` | `30` | Seconds until retry |\n| `X-RateLimit-Limit` | `100` | Max requests |\n| `X-RateLimit-Remaining` | `0` | Remaining requests |\n| `X-RateLimit-Reset` | epoch | Reset timestamp |\n\n## Kong Rate Limiting Plugin\n\n```bash\ncurl -X POST http://localhost:8001/services/{id}/plugins \\\n  -d \"name=rate-limiting\" \\\n  -d \"config.minute=100\" \\\n  -d \"config.policy=redis\" \\\n  -d \"config.redis_host=redis\"\n```\n\n### References\n\n- Redis Rate Limiting: https://redis.io/glossary/rate-limiting/\n- IETF RateLimit Headers: https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-ietf-httpapi-ratelimit-headers/\n- Kong Rate Limiting: https://docs.konghq.com/hub/kong-inc/rate-limiting/\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

Security\n\nVerify that sensitive data is encrypted during transmission.\n\n```bash\n# Check TLS configuration\n# Using testssl.sh\n./testssl.sh \"https://target.example.com\"\n\n# Quick TLS checks URLs are logged in browser history, server logs, proxy logs, Referer headers\n# Look for: /login?username=admin&password=secret\n# /api/data?ssn=123-45-6789\n# /search?credit_card=4111111111111111\n\n# Check WebSocket encryption\ncurl -s \"https://target.example.com/\" | grep -oP \"(ws|wss)://[^\\\"'> ]+\"\n# ws:// is unencrypted; should only use wss://\n```\n\n### Step 4: Examine Browser Storage for Sensitive Data\n\nCheck local storage, session storage, cookies, and cached responses.\n\n```bash\n# Check what cookies are set and their security attributes\ncurl -s -I \"https://target.example.com/login\" | grep -i \"set-cookie\"\n\n# In browser DevTools (Application tab):\n# 1. Local Storage: Check for stored tokens, PII, credentials\n# 2. Session Storage: Check for temporary sensitive data\n# 3. IndexedDB: Check for cached application data\n# 4. Cache Storage: Check for cached API responses containing PII\n# 5. Cookies: Check for sensitive data in cookie values\n\n# Common insecure storage patterns:\n# localStorage.setItem('access_token', 'eyJ...');  // XSS can steal\n# localStorage.setItem('user', JSON.stringify({email: '...', ssn: '...'}));\n# sessionStorage.setItem('credit_card', '4111...');\n\n# Check for autocomplete on sensitive forms\ncurl -s \"https://target.example.com/login\" | \\\n  grep -oP '<input[^>]*(password|credit|ssn|card)[^>]*>' | \\\n  grep -v 'autocomplete=\"off\"'\n# Password and credit card fields should have autocomplete=\"off\"\n\n# Check Cache-Control headers on sensitive pages\nfor page in /account/profile /api/users/me /transactions /billing; do\n  echo -n \"$page: \"\n  curl -s -I \"https://target.example.com$page\" \\\n    -H \"Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN\" | \\\n    grep -i \"cache-control\" | tr -d '\\r'\n  echo\ndone\n# Sensitive pages should have: Cache-Control: no-store\n```\n\n### Step 5: Scan Git Repositories and Source Code for Secrets\n\nSearch for accidentally committed secrets in version control.\n\n```bash\n# Check for exposed .git directory\ncurl -s \"https://target.example.com/.git/config\"\ncurl -s \"https://target.example.com/.git/HEAD\"\n\n# If .git is exposed, use git-dumper to download\n# pip install git-dumper\ngit-dumper https://target.example.com/.git /tmp/target-repo\n\n# Scan downloaded repository with trufflehog\ntrufflehog filesystem /tmp/target-repo\n\n# Scan with gitleaks\ngitleaks detect --source /tmp/target-repo -v\n\n# If GitHub/GitLab repository is available (authorized scope)\ntrufflehog github --org target-organization --token $GITHUB_TOKEN\ngitleaks detect --source https://github.com/org/repo -v\n\n# Common secrets found in repositories:\n# - AWS access keys (AKIA...)\n# - Database connection strings\n# - API keys (Google, Stripe, Twilio, SendGrid)\n# - Private SSH keys\n# - JWT signing secrets\n# - OAuth client secrets\n# - SMTP credentials\n\n# Search for secrets in Docker images\n# docker save target-image:latest | tar x -C /tmp/docker-layers\n# Search each layer for credentials\n```\n\n### Step 6: Test Data Masking and Redaction\n\nVerify that sensitive data is properly masked in the application.\n\n```bash\n# Check if credit card numbers are fully displayed\ncurl -s -H \"Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN\" \\\n  \"https://target.example.com/api/payment-methods\" | jq .\n# Should show: **** **** **** 4242, not full number\n\n# Check if SSN/national ID is masked\ncurl -s -H \"Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN\" \\\n  \"https://target.example.com/api/users/me\" | jq '.ssn'\n# Should show: ***-**-6789, not full SSN\n\n# Check API responses for password hashes\ncurl -s -H \"Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN\" \\\n  \"https://target.example.com/api/users\" | jq '.[].password // empty'\n# Should return nothing; password hashes should never be in API responses\n\n# Check export/download features for unmasked data\ncurl -s -H \"Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN\" \\\n  \"https://target.example.com/api/users/export?format=csv\" | head -5\n# CSV exports often contain unmasked PII\n\n# Check logging endpoints for sensitive data\ncurl -s -H \"Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN\" \\\n  \"https://target.example.com/api/admin/logs\" | \\\n  grep -iE \"(password|token|secret|credit_card|ssn)\" | head -10\n# Logs should not contain sensitive data in plaintext\n\n# Test for sensitive data in error messages\ncurl -s -X POST \\\n  -H \"Content-Type: application/json\" \\\n  -d '{\"email\":\"duplicate@test.com\"}' \\\n  \"https://target.example.com/api/register\"\n# Should not reveal: \"User with email duplicate@test.com already exists\"\n# Should show: \"Registration failed\" (generic)\n```\n\n## Key Concepts\n\n| Concept | Description |\n|---------|-------------|\n| **Sensitive Data Exposure** | Unintended disclosure of PII, credentials, financial data, or health records |\n| **Data Over-Exposure** | API returning more data fields than the client needs |\n| **Secret Leakage** | API keys, tokens, or credentials exposed in client-side code or logs |\n| **Data at Rest** | Sensitive data stored in databases, files, or backups without encryption |\n| **Data in Transit** | Sensitive data transmitted over network without TLS encryption |\n| **Data Masking** | Replacing sensitive data with redacted values (e.g., showing last 4 digits of credit card) |\n| **PII** | Personally Identifiable Information - data that can identify an individual |\n| **Information Leakage** | Excessive error messages, stack traces, or debug information in responses |\n\n## Tools & Systems\n\n| Tool | Purpose |\n|------|---------|\n| **Burp Suite Professional** | Response analysis and regex-based sensitive data scanning |\n| **trufflehog** | Secret detection across git repos, filesystems, and cloud storage |\n| **gitleaks** | Git repository scanning for hardcoded secrets |\n| **testssl.sh** | TLS/SSL configuration assessment |\n| **git-dumper** | Downloading exposed .git directories from web servers |\n| **SecretFinder** | JavaScript file analysis for exposed API keys and tokens |\n| **Retire.js** | Detecting JavaScript libraries with known vulnerabilities |\n\n## Common Scenarios\n\n### Scenario 1: API Key in JavaScript Bundle\nThe application's JavaScript bundle contains a hardcoded Google Maps API key and a Stripe publishable key. The Stripe key has overly broad permissions, allowing the attacker to create charges.\n\n### Scenario 2: User API Returns Password Hashes\nThe `/api/users` endpoint returns complete user objects including bcrypt password hashes. Attackers can extract hashes and attempt offline cracking.\n\n### Scenario 3: PII in Cached API Responses\nThe user profile API endpoint returns full SSN and credit card numbers without masking. The endpoint does not set `Cache-Control: no-store`, so responses are cached in the browser and proxy caches.\n\n### Scenario 4: Git Repository with Database Credentials\nThe `.git` directory is accessible on the production server. Using git-dumper, the attacker downloads the repository history, finding database credentials committed in an early commit that were later \"removed\" but remain in git history.\n\n## Output Format\n\n```\n## Sensitive Data Exposure Assessment Report\n\n**Target**: target.example.com\n**Assessment Date**: 2024-01-15\n**OWASP Category**: A02:2021 - Cryptographic Failures\n\n### Findings Summary\n| Finding | Severity | Data Type |\n|---------|----------|-----------|\n| API keys in JavaScript source | High | Credentials |\n| Password hashes in API response | Critical | Authentication |\n| Unmasked SSN in user profile | Critical | PII |\n| Credit card number in export | High | Financial |\n| .git directory exposed | Critical | Source code + secrets |\n| Missing TLS on API endpoint | High | All data in transit |\n| Sensitive data in error messages | Medium | Technical info |\n\n### Critical: Exposed Secrets\n| Secret Type | Location | Risk |\n|-------------|----------|------|\n| AWS Access Key (AKIA...) | /static/app.js line 342 | AWS resource access |\n| Stripe Secret Key (sk_live_...) | .env (via .git exposure) | Payment processing |\n| Database URL with credentials | .git history commit abc123 | Database access |\n| JWT Signing Secret | config.json (via .git) | Token forgery |\n\n### Data Over-Exposure in APIs\n| Endpoint | Unnecessary Fields Returned |\n|----------|-----------------------------|\n| GET /api/users | password_hash, internal_id, created_ip |\n| GET /api/users/{id} | ssn, credit_card_full, date_of_birth |\n| GET /api/orders | customer_phone, customer_address |\n\n### Recommendation\n1. Remove all hardcoded secrets from client-side code; use backend proxies\n2. Rotate all exposed credentials immediately\n3. Remove .git directory from production web root\n4. Implement response field filtering; return only required fields\n5. Mask sensitive data (SSN, credit card) in all API responses\n6. Add Cache-Control: no-store to all sensitive endpoints\n7. Enable TLS 1.2+ on all endpoints; redirect HTTP to HTTPS\n8. Implement secret scanning in CI/CD pipeline (trufflehog/gitleaks)\n```\n/root/.codex/skills/testing-for-sensitive-data-exposure/references/api-reference.md\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

security headers including CSP, HSTS, X-Frame-Options,\n  and cookie attributes to identify missing or misconfigured browser-level protections.\ndomain: cybersecurity\nsubdomain: web-application-security\ntags:\n- penetration-testing\n- security-headers\n- csp\n- hsts\n- owasp\n- web-security\n- hardening\nversion: '1.0'\nauthor: mahipal\nlicense: Apache-2.0\nnist_csf:\n- PR.PS-01\n- ID.RA-01\n- PR.DS-10\n- DE.CM-01\nmitre_attack:\n- T1190\n- T1059.007\n- T1505.003\n- T1083\n---\n\n# Performing Security Headers Audit\n\n## When to Use\n\n- During authorized web application security assessments as a standard configuration review\n- When evaluating browser-level protections against XSS, clickjacking, and data leakage\n- For compliance assessments requiring security header implementation (PCI DSS, SOC 2)\n- When performing initial reconnaissance to identify easy-win security improvements\n- During CI/CD pipeline security gate checks for new deployments\n\n## Prerequisites\n\n- **Authorization**: Written scope for the target application (header review is low-risk)\n- **curl**: For fetching response headers from target endpoints\n- **SecurityHeaders.com**: Online scanner for quick header assessment\n- **Mozilla Observatory**: Mozilla's web security testing tool\n- **Burp Suite**: For comprehensive header analysis across multiple pages\n- **Browser DevTools**: For examining headers and CSP violations in real-time\n\n## Workflow\n\n### Step 1: Collect Security Headers from Target\n\nRetrieve and catalog all security-related response headers.\n\n```bash\n# Fetch all response headers\ncurl -s -I \"https://target.example.com/\" | grep -iE \\\n  \"(strict-transport|content-security|x-frame|x-content-type|x-xss|referrer-policy|permissions-policy|feature-policy|x-permitted|cross-origin|set-cookie|server|x-powered-by|cache-control)\"\n\n# Check headers across multiple pages\nPAGES=(\"/\" \"/login\" \"/api/health\" \"/admin\" \"/account/settings\" \"/static/app.js\")\n\nfor page in \"${PAGES[@]}\"; do\n  echo \"=== $page ===\"\n  curl -s -I \"https://target.example.com$page\" 2>/dev/null | grep -iE \\\n    \"(strict-transport|content-security|x-frame|x-content-type|x-xss|referrer-policy|permissions-policy|set-cookie|server|x-powered)\"\n  echo\ndone\n\n# Check both HTTP and HTTPS responses\necho \"=== HTTP Response ===\"\ncurl -s -I \"http://target.example.com/\" | head -20\necho \"=== HTTPS Response ===\"\ncurl -s -I \"https://target.example.com/\" | head -20\n```\n\n### Step 2: Assess Transport Security (HSTS)\n\nEvaluate HTTP Strict Transport Security configuration.\n\n```bash\n# Check HSTS header\ncurl -s -I \"https://target.example.com/\" | grep -i \"strict-transport-security\"\n\n# Expected: Strict-Transport-Security: max-age=31536000; includeSubDomains; preload\n\n# Verify HSTS attributes:\n# max-age: Should be >= 31536000 (1 year) for preload eligibility\n# includeSubDomains: Protects all subdomains\n# preload: Eligible for browser HSTS preload list\n\n# Check if HTTP redirects to HTTPS\ncurl -s -I \"http://target.example.com/\" | head -5\n# Should be 301/302 redirect to https://\n\n# Check if HSTS is on the preload list\n# Visit: https://hstspreload.org/?domain=target.example.com\n\n# Test for HTTPS-only cookies\ncurl -s -I \"https://target.example.com/login\" | grep -i \"set-cookie\"\n# All session cookies should have Secure flag\n\n# Check for mixed content\ncurl -s \"https://target.example.com/\" | grep -oP \"http://[^\\\"']+\" | head -20\n# HTTP resources loaded on HTTPS pages create mixed content vulnerabilities\n```\n\n### Step 3: Audit Content Security Policy (CSP)\n\nAnalyze CSP headers for effectiveness and potential bypasses.\n\n```bash\n# Extract CSP header\nCSP=$(curl -s -I \"https://target.example.com/\" | grep -i \"content-security-policy\" | cut -d: -f2-)\necho \"$CSP\"\n\n# Check for dangerous directives:\n# 'unsafe-inline' in script-src: Allows inline scripts (XSS risk)\n# 'unsafe-eval' in script-src: Allows eval() (XSS risk)\n# * in any directive: Allows loading from any origin\n# data: in script-src: Allows data: URI scripts\n# Missing default-src: No fallback policy\n\necho \"$CSP\" | tr ';' '\\n' | while read directive; do\n  echo \"  $directive\"\n  if echo \"$directive\" | grep -q \"unsafe-inline\"; then\n    echo \"    WARNING: unsafe-inline allows inline script execution\"\n  fi\n  if echo \"$directive\" | grep -q \"unsafe-eval\"; then\n    echo \"    WARNING: unsafe-eval allows eval() calls\"\n  fi\n  if echo \"$directive\" | grep -q \" \\* \"; then\n    echo \"    WARNING: wildcard allows loading from any origin\"\n  fi\ndone\n\n# Check for CSP report-only (not enforcing)\ncurl -s -I \"https://target.example.com/\" | grep -i \"content-security-policy-report-only\"\n# Report-only does NOT block violations, only logs them\n\n# Test CSP with Google's evaluator\n# https://csp-evaluator.withgoogle.com/\n# Paste the CSP header for automated analysis\n\n# Check for CSP bypass via whitelisted domains\n# If CDN domains are whitelisted, check for JSONP endpoints or angular libraries\n```\n\n### Step 4: Check Frame Protection and Click Defense Headers\n\nVerify anti-clickjacking and iframe embedding controls.\n\n```bash\n# X-Frame-Options\ncurl -s -I \"https://target.example.com/\" | grep -i \"x-frame-options\"\n# Expected: DENY or SAMEORIGIN\n# ALLOW-FROM is deprecated and not supported in modern browsers\n\n# CSP frame-ancestors (supersedes X-Frame-Options)\ncurl -s -I \"https://target.example.com/\" | grep -i \"content-security-policy\" | grep -o \"frame-ancestors[^;]*\"\n# Expected: frame-ancestors 'none' or frame-ancestors 'self'\n\n# X-Content-Type-Options\ncurl -s -I \"https://target.example.com/\" | grep -i \"x-content-type-options\"\n# Expected: nosniff (prevents MIME type sniffing)\n\n# X-XSS-Protection (legacy, but still useful for older browsers)\ncurl -s -I \"https://target.example.com/\" | grep -i \"x-xss-protection\"\n# Expected: 1; mode=block (or 0 if CSP is comprehensive)\n# Note: Modern recommendation is 0 (disable) when CSP is present\n\n# Referrer-Policy\ncurl -s -I \"https://target.example.com/\" | grep -i \"referrer-policy\"\n# Expected: strict-origin-when-cross-origin or no-referrer\n# Prevents sensitive URL data from leaking via Referer header\n```\n\n### Step 5: Audit Cookie Security Attributes\n\nExamine session and authentication cookies for security flags.\n\n```bash\n# Fetch all Set-Cookie headers\ncurl -s -I -L \"https://target.example.com/login\" | grep -i \"set-cookie\"\n\n# Check each cookie for required attributes:\n# Secure: Only sent over HTTPS\n# HttpOnly: Not accessible via JavaScript (prevents XSS cookie theft)\n# SameSite: Controls cross-site cookie sending (Strict, Lax, None)\n# Path: Restricts cookie scope\n# Domain: Controls which domains receive the cookie\n# Max-Age/Expires: Cookie lifetime\n\n# Automated cookie check\ncurl -s -I \"https://target.example.com/login\" | grep -i \"set-cookie\" | while read line; do\n  echo \"Cookie: $(echo \"$line\" | grep -oP '[^:]+=[^;]+')\"\n  missing=\"\"\n  echo \"$line\" | grep -qi \"secure\" || missing=\"$missing Secure\"\n  echo \"$line\" | grep -qi \"httponly\" || missing=\"$missing HttpOnly\"\n  echo \"$line\" | grep -qi \"samesite\" || missing=\"$missing SameSite\"\n  if [ -n \"$missing\" ]; then\n    echo \"  MISSING:$missing\"\n  else\n    echo \"  All flags present\"\n  fi\ndone\n\n# Check for __Host- and __Secure- cookie prefixes\n# __Host- cookies must have Secure, Path=/, no Domain\n# __Secure- cookies must have Secure flag\n```\n\n### Step 6: Check Permissions Policy and Information Disclosure\n\nReview browser feature controls and information leakage headers.\n\n```bash\n# Permissions-Policy (formerly Feature-Policy)\ncurl -s -I \"https://target.example.com/\" | grep -i \"permissions-policy\"\n# Controls browser features: camera, microphone, geolocation, etc.\n# Expected: Restrict unused features\n# Example: permissions-policy: camera=(), microphone=(), geolocation=()\n\n# Cross-Origin headers\ncurl -s -I \"https://target.example.com/\" | grep -iE \"(cross-origin-embedder|cross-origin-opener|cross-origin-resource)\"\n# COEP: Cross-Origin-Embedder-Policy: require-corp\n# COOP: Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin\n# CORP: Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-origin\n\n# Information disclosure headers to flag\ncurl -s -I \"https://target.example.com/\" | grep -iE \"(server|x-powered-by|x-aspnet|x-generator)\"\n# Server: Apache/2.4.52 (should be removed or generic)\n# X-Powered-By: PHP/8.1.2 (should be removed)\n# These headers reveal technology stack to attackers\n\n# Cache-Control for sensitive pages\ncurl -s -I \"https://target.example.com/account/settings\" | grep -i \"cache-control\"\n# Sensitive pages should have: Cache-Control: no-store, no-cache, must-revalidate\n# Prevents browser caching of sensitive data\n\n# Generate comprehensive report using online tools\necho \"Scan with SecurityHeaders.com: https://securityheaders.com/?q=target.example.com\"\necho \"Scan with Mozilla Observatory: https://observatory.mozilla.org/analyze/target.example.com\"\n```\n\n## Key Concepts\n\n| Concept | Description |\n|---------|-------------|\n| **HSTS** | Forces browsers to only use HTTPS for the domain, preventing protocol downgrade attacks |\n| **CSP** | Restricts which resources (scripts, styles, images) can load on the page |\n| **X-Frame-Options** | Controls whether the page can be embedded in iframes (clickjacking defense) |\n| **X-Content-Type-Options** | Prevents MIME type sniffing; forces browser to respect declared Content-Type |\n| **Referrer-Policy** | Controls how much referrer information is sent with cross-origin requests |\n| **Permissions-Policy** | Restricts browser features (camera, microphone, geolocation) available to the page |\n| **SameSite Cookie** | Controls when cookies are sent in cross-site contexts (Strict, Lax, None) |\n| **HSTS Preloading** | Hardcoding HSTS policy in browser source code for first-visit protection |\n\n## Tools & Systems\n\n| Tool | Purpose |\n|------|---------|\n| **SecurityHeaders.com** | Online scanner providing letter-grade security header assessment |\n| **Mozilla Observatory** | Comprehensive web security scanner with scoring and recommendations |\n| **CSP Evaluator (Google)** | Analyzes Content Security Policy for weaknesses and bypasses |\n| **Burp Suite Professional** | Inspecting response headers across all application pages |\n| **securityheaders (CLI)** | Command-line security header scanner |\n| **Hardenize** | TLS and security header monitoring service |\n\n## Common Scenarios\n\n### Scenario 1: Complete Header Absence\nA legacy application returns no security headers at all. No HSTS, CSP, X-Frame-Options, or cookie security flags. Every page is vulnerable to clickjacking, XSS has no browser-level mitigation, and cookies are sent over HTTP.\n\n### Scenario 2: Weak CSP with unsafe-inline\nThe CSP header includes `script-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'`. While it restricts external script loading, the `unsafe-inline` directive allows any inline script to execute, rendering the CSP ineffective against XSS.\n\n### Scenario 3: Session Cookie Without Secure Flag\nThe session cookie is set without the `Secure` flag. On mixed HTTP/HTTPS sites, the session token can be intercepted by a network attacker via a plain HTTP request.\n\n### Scenario 4: Missing HSTS Enabling SSL Stripping\nNo HSTS header is present. An attacker on the network can perform an SSL stripping attack, downgrading the victim's HTTPS connection to HTTP and intercepting all traffic.\n\n## Output Format\n\n```\n## Security Headers Audit Report\n\n**Target**: target.example.com\n**Grade**: D (SecurityHeaders.com)\n**Assessment Date**: 2024-01-15\n\n### Headers Assessment\n| Header | Status | Current Value | Recommended |\n|--------|--------|---------------|-------------|\n| Strict-Transport-Security | MISSING | - | max-age=31536000; includeSubDomains; preload |\n| Content-Security-Policy | WEAK | script-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline' | script-src 'self' 'nonce-{random}' |\n| X-Frame-Options | MISSING | - | DENY |\n| X-Content-Type-Options | PRESENT | nosniff | nosniff (OK) |\n| Referrer-Policy | MISSING | - | strict-origin-when-cross-origin |\n| Permissions-Policy | MISSING | - | camera=(), microphone=(), geolocation=() |\n| X-XSS-Protection | MISSING | - | 0 (with strong CSP) |\n\n### Cookie Security\n| Cookie | Secure | HttpOnly | SameSite | Path |\n|--------|--------|----------|----------|------|\n| session | NO | YES | Not set | / |\n| user_pref | NO | NO | Not set | / |\n| csrf_token | YES | NO | Strict | / |\n\n### Information Disclosure\n| Header | Value | Risk |\n|--------|-------|------|\n| Server | Apache/2.4.52 | Technology fingerprinting |\n| X-Powered-By | PHP/8.1.2 | Version-specific exploit targeting |\n\n### Recommendation Priority\n1. **Critical**: Add Secure and SameSite flags to session cookie\n2. **High**: Implement HSTS with min 1-year max-age\n3. **High**: Replace 'unsafe-inline' in CSP with nonce-based policy\n4. **Medium**: Add X-Frame-Options: DENY\n5. **Medium**: Add Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin\n6. **Low**: Remove Server and X-Powered-By version information\n7. **Low**: Add Permissions-Policy to restrict unused browser features\n```\n/root/.codex/skills/performing-security-headers-audit/references/api-reference.md\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

    Harness.Fixture timing = Harness.freshFixture(\"auth-timing\");\n        Harness.Fixture upgrade = Harness.freshFixture(\"auth-upgrade\");\n        Harness.Fixture malformed = malformedLoginsConsumeBudget(malformed);\n        lockoutExpires(recovery);\n        equalWork(timing);\n        upgradeOnWeakCredential(upgrade);\n\n        System.exit(Harness.report(\"AuthServiceTest\"));\n    }\n\n    private static void happyPath(Harness.Fixture fixture) throws Exception {\n        Harness.section(\"a correct credential is verified Harness.PW_ONE);\n        Harness.check(byName.authenticated(), \"sign-in works with the username too\");\n        AuthService.Result byCaseFoldedEmail = Harness.login(fixture, \"SIGNIN@CORP.EXAMPLE\", Harness.PW_ONE);\n        Harness.check(byCaseFoldedEmail.authenticated(),\n            \"the mailbox branch reaches the same account through the documented canonical form\");\n\n        fixture.close();\n    }\n\n    /** Every refusal answers with one sentence, and none of them says which branch fired. */\n    private static void uniformFailures(Harness.Fixture fixture) throws Exception {\n        Harness.section(\"every sign-in refusal is the same sentence\");\n        Harness.registerStatus(fixture, EMAIL, NAME, Harness.PW_ONE, \"src-u\");\n\n        List<String> messages = new ArrayList<>();\n        List<AuthService.Status> statuses = new ArrayList<>();\n\n        // 1. malformed identifier (would previously say \"no such account\" in a naive design)\n        statuses.add(Harness.login(fixture, \"not-an-address-or-name\", Harness.PW_WRONG).status());\n        messages.add(Harness.login(fixture, \"not-an-address-or-name\", Harness.PW_WRONG).message());\n        // 2. identifier that is a valid email shape but no such account\n        statuses.add(Harness.login(fixture, \"ghost@corp.example\", Harness.PW_WRONG).status());\n        messages.add(Harness.login(fixture, \"ghost@corp.example\", Harness.PW_WRONG).message());\n        // 3. identifier that is a valid username shape but no such account\n        statuses.add(Harness.login(fixture, \"ghost-user\", Harness.PW_WRONG).status());\n        messages.add(Harness.login(fixture, \"ghost-user\", Harness.PW_WRONG).message());\n        // 4. real account, wrong password\n        statuses.add(Harness.login(fixture, EMAIL, Harness.PW_WRONG).status());\n        messages.add(Harness.login(fixture, EMAIL, Harness.PW_WRONG).message());\n        // 5. real username, wrong password\n        statuses.add(Harness.login(fixture, NAME, Harness.PW_WRONG).status());\n        messages.add(Harness.login(fixture, NAME, Harness.PW_WRONG).message());\n        // 6. empty password\n        statuses.add(Harness.login(fixture, EMAIL, \"\").status());\n        messages.add(Harness.login(fixture, EMAIL, \"\").message());\n        // 7. oversized password (never allowed to reach the KDF)\n        String oversized = \"a1\" + \"z\".repeat(600);\n        statuses.add(Harness.login(fixture, EMAIL, oversized).status());\n        messages.add(Harness.login(fixture, EMAIL, oversized).message());\n        // 8. trailing invisible character on a real username - must not fold onto the account\n        statuses.add(Harness.login(fixture, NAME + \"\\t\", Harness.PW_ONE).status());\n        messages.add(Harness.login(fixture, NAME + \"\\t\", Harness.PW_ONE).message());\n\n        Harness.requireNotEmpty(messages, \"the refusal capture\");\n        Harness.atLeast(messages.size(), 8L, \"at least eight distinct refusal paths were exercised\");\n        for (String message : messages) {\n            Harness.equal(message, AuthService.INVALID_MESSAGE,\n                \"the refusal is the shared invalid-credential sentence\");\n        }\n        Harness.check(messages.stream().distinct().count() == 1,\n            \"all \" + messages.size() + \" refusals are byte-identical\");\n        Harness.check(!AuthService.INVALID_MESSAGE.toLowerCase().contains(\"exist\")\n                && !AuthService.INVALID_MESSAGE.toLowerCase().contains(\"unknown\")\n                && !AuthService.INVALID_MESSAGE.toLowerCase().contains(\"lock\")\n                && !AuthService.INVALID_MESSAGE.toLowerCase().contains(\"throttl\")\n                && !AuthService.INVALID_MESSAGE.toLowerCase().contains(\"attempt\")\n                && !AuthService.INVALID_MESSAGE.toLowerCase().contains(\"invalid\"),\n            \"the refusal wording names no branch: \" + AuthService.INVALID_MESSAGE);\n        Harness.check(!AuthService.INVALID_MESSAGE.contains(EMAIL) && !AuthService.INVALID_MESSAGE.contains(NAME),\n            \"the refusal does not echo   // A trailing tab on a real username must be refused, not silently trimmed onto the account.\n        Harness.check(statuses.get(7) != AuthService.Status.AUTHENTICATED,\n            \"a trailing tab Harness.check(joined(lines).contains(\"LOGIN_FAILURE\"),\n            \"the trail distinguishes a credential mismatch even though the client cannot\");\n        Harness.check(joined(lines).contains(\"no such account\"),\n            \"the trail records the unknown-account branch server-side\");\n        Harness.check(joined(lines).contains(\"LOGIN_REFUSED_INVALID_INPUT\"),\n            \"the trail records the malformed-input branch server-side\");\n        Harness.check(!joined(lines).contains(EMAIL) && !joined(lines).contains(NAME)\n                && !joined(lines).contains(Harness.PW_WRONG),\n            \"the trail holds tags only, never the identifiers or the candidate password\");\n        fixture.close();\n    }\n\n    private static void accountLockout(Harness.Fixture fixture) throws Exception {\n        Harness.section(\"a wrong-password burst locks the account temporarily, not permanently\");\n        Harness.registerStatus(fixture, EMAIL, NAME, Harness.PW_ONE, \"src-a\");\n\n        int refused = 0;\n        for (int attempt = 0; attempt < Throttle.ACCOUNT_MAX_FAILURES - 1; attempt++) {\n            AuthService.Result result = Harness.loginFrom(fixture, EMAIL, Harness.PW_WRONG,\n                \"burst-\" + attempt);\n            Harness.equal(result.message(), AuthService.INVALID_MESSAGE,\n                \"a strike inside the budget answers with the shared sentence\");\n            if (result.status() != AuthService.Status.INVALID_CREDENTIALS) {\n                refused++;\n            }\n        }\n        Harness.equal(refused, 0, \"no earlier attempt was refused by the account budget\");\n\n        AuthService.Result lastStrike = Harness.loginFrom(fixture, EMAIL, Harness.PW_WRONG, \"burst-final\");\n        Harness.equal(lastStrike.message(), AuthService.INVALID_MESSAGE,\n            \"the attempt that trips the lock still answers generically\");\n\n        AuthService.Result locked = Harness.loginFrom(fixture, EMAIL, Harness.PW_ONE, \"burst-owner\");\n        Harness.equal(locked.status(), AuthService.Status.THROTTLED,\n            \"the correct password is refused while the account is in its window - fail closed\");\n        Harness.equal(locked.message(), AuthService.INVALID_MESSAGE,\n            \"and the refusal is still the shared sentence, so a lockout is not observable\");\n        Harness.check(locked.session() == null, \"no session is issued while the account is locked\");\n\n        List<String> lines = Harness.auditLines(fixture);\n        Harness.check(joined(lines).contains(\"ACCOUNT_LOCKED\"),\n            \"the lockout itself is recorded\");\n        Harness.check(joined(lines).contains(\"LOGIN_THROTTLED_ACCOUNT\"),\n            \"the refusal-by-lockout is distinguishable in the trail only\");\n        Harness.note(\"the account budget is \" + Throttle.ACCOUNT_MAX_FAILURES + \" failures inside \"\n            + Throttle.ACCOUNT_WINDOW_SECONDS + \"s, locking for \" + Throttle.ACCOUNT_LOCK_SECONDS + \"s\");\n        fixture.close();\n    }\n\n    private static void sourceBudget(Harness.Fixture fixture) throws Exception {\n        Harness.section(\"attempts are also limited per source, so one budget cannot starve the other\");\n        Harness.registerStatus(fixture, EMAIL, NAME, Harness.PW_ONE, \"src-s\");\n\n        int served = 0;\n        int throttled = 0;\n        // All attempts come from ONE source against DIFFERENT accounts, so the per-account counter\n        // can never be what stops them - only the source budget can.\n        for (int attempt = 0; attempt < Throttle.SOURCE_MAX_ATTEMPTS + 3; attempt++) {\n            AuthService.Result result = Harness.loginFrom(fixture, \"probe\" + attempt + \"@corp.example\",\n                Harness.PW_WRONG, \"one-source\");\n            if (result.status() == AuthService.Status.THROTTLED) {\n                throttled++;\n            } else {\n                served++;\n            }\n            Harness.equal(result.message(), AuthService.INVALID_MESSAGE,\n                \"a source-throttled refusal is worded like every other refusal\");\n        }\n        Harness.equal(served, Throttle.SOURCE_MAX_ATTEMPTS,\n            \"exactly the source budget is served before refusal\");\n        Harness.atLeast(throttled, 1L, \"the spent source budget refuses further attempts\");\n\n        // The decisive property: a spent source budget must not lock the legitimate owner out,\n        // because the two budgets are separate namespaces.\n        Harness.Fixture other = Harness.freshFixture(\"auth-source-other\");\n        Harness.registerStatus(other, \"owner@corp.example\", \"owner-user\", Harness.PW_ONE, \"src-o\");\n        Harness.check(Harness.loginFrom(other, \"owner@corp.example\", Harness.PW_ONE, \"another-source\")\n            .authenticated(), \"a different source is unaffected by the spent budget - the limit is per source\");\n        other.close();\n\n        fixture.clock().advanceSeconds(Throttle.SOURCE_WINDOW_SECONDS);\n        Harness.check(Harness.loginFrom(fixture, \"probe-after@corp.example\", Harness.PW_WRONG, \"one-source\")\n            .status() != AuthService.Status.THROTTLED, \"the source window refills and service resumes\");\n        fixture.close();\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Malformed sign-in attempts must cost the caller the same budget as well-formed ones. Before\n     * this, an unusable identifier short-circuited before the source consume - so an attacker could\n     * make the server run a full key-derivation and write an audit record an unlimited number of times\n     * with input that never touched the limit. The per-source budget is worth nothing if the most\n     * expensive path is the one it lets through for free.\n     */\n    private static void malformedLoginsConsumeBudget(Harness.Fixture fixture) throws Exception {\n        Harness.section(\"malformed sign-in input is rate limited too, so it is not a free work amplifier\");\n        Harness.registerStatus(fixture, EMAIL, NAME, Harness.PW_ONE, \"src-mal\");\n\n        int served = 0;\n        int throttled = 0;\n        // \"!!broken!!\" carries no '@' and is not a valid username, so it is rejected by the input\n        // policy before any credential is compared - the exact path that used to bypass the consume.\n        for (int attempt = 0; attempt < Throttle.SOURCE_MAX_ATTEMPTS + 3; attempt++) {\n            AuthService.Result result = Harness.loginFrom(fixture, \"!!broken!!\", Harness.PW_WRONG,\n                \"bad-source\");\n            if (result.status() == AuthService.Status.THROTTLED) {\n                throttled++;\n            } else {\n                served++;\n                Harness.equal(AuthService.Status.INVALID_CREDENTIALS, result.status(),\n                    \"while the budget lasts, malformed input gets the ordinary generic refusal\");\n            }\n            Harness.equal(result.message(), AuthService.INVALID_MESSAGE,\n                \"a malformed refusal, a throttled refusal and a wrong password are worded identically\");\n        }\n        Harness.equal(served, Throttle.SOURCE_MAX_ATTEMPTS,\n            \"exactly the source budget of malformed attempts is served before refusal - the consume is\"\n                + \" charged on the rejected-input path, not skipped\");\n        Harness.atLeast((long) throttled, 1L,\n            \"once spent, malformed attempts are throttled like any other, so the limit bounds the\"\n                + \" expensive derivation path\");\n        Harness.check(Harness.auditLines(fixture).stream().anyMatch(line -> line.contains(\"LOGIN_THROTTLED_SOURCE\")),\n            \"the source-denial of a malformed flood is on the record\");\n\n        Harness.Fixture other = Harness.freshFixture(\"auth-malformed-other\");\n        Harness.registerStatus(other, \"owner@corp.example\", \"owner-user\", Harness.PW_ONE, \"src-o2\");\n        Harness.check(Harness.loginFrom(other, \"owner@corp.example\", Harness.PW_ONE, \"clean-source\")\n            .authenticated(), \"a spent source cannot deny a different source - the flood is per source\");\n        other.close();\n\n        fixture.clock().advanceSeconds(Throttle.SOURCE_WINDOW_SECONDS + 1L);\n        Harness.check(Harness.loginFrom(fixture, \"!!broken!!\", Harness.PW_WRONG, \"bad-source\").status()\n                != AuthService.Status.THROTTLED,\n            \"after the window malformed input is served again - bounded, never permanent\");\n        fixture.close();\n    }\n\n    private static void lockoutExpires(Harness.Fixture fixture) throws Exception {\n        Harness.section(\"the lockout expires and a success clears the counter - it is not a lock lever\");\n        Harness.registerStatus(fixture, EMAIL, NAME, Harness.PW_ONE, \"src-r\");\n\n        for (int attempt = 0; attempt < Throttle.ACCOUNT_MAX_FAILURES; attempt++) {\n            Harness.loginFrom(fixture, EMAIL, Harness.PW_WRONG, \"strike-\" + attempt);\n        }\n        Harness.equal(Harness.loginFrom(fixture, EMAIL, Harness.PW_ONE, \"owner-locked\").status(),\n            AuthService.Status.THROTTLED, \"the account is locked after the burst\");\n\n        fixture.clock().advanceSeconds(Throttle.ACCOUNT_LOCK_SECONDS + 1);\n        AuthService.Result after = Harness.loginFrom(fixture, EMAIL, Harness.PW_ONE, \"owner-after\");\n        Harness.equal(after.status(), AuthService.Status.AUTHENTICATED,\n            \"the window is temporary - the owner is served again once it passes\");\n        Harness.check(after.session() != null, \"and the recovered sign-in really issued a session\");\n\n        List<String> lines = Harness.auditLines(fixture);\n        Harness.check(joined(lines).contains(\"ACCOUNT_UNLOCKED\"),\n            \"the trail shows the strike window was cleared by a successful sign-in\");\n\n        // A success must reset the counter, otherwise four strikes now plus one later would lock\n        // the account out on the fifth attempt across an unrelated gap.\n        Harness.Fixture clear = Harness.freshFixture(\"auth-clear\");\n        Harness.registerStatus(clear, EMAIL, NAME, Harness.PW_ONE, \"src-c\");\n        for (int attempt = 0; attempt < Throttle.ACCOUNT_MAX_FAILURES - 1; attempt++) {\n            Harness.loginFrom(clear, EMAIL, Harness.PW_WRONG, \"pre-\" + attempt);\n        }\n        Harness.check(Harness.loginFrom(clear, EMAIL, Harness.PW_ONE, \"owner-mid\").authenticated(),\n            \"the owner signs in just below the strike threshold\");\n        for (int attempt = 0; attempt < Throttle.ACCOUNT_MAX_FAILURES - 1; attempt++) {\n            Harness.loginFrom(clear, EMAIL, Harness.PW_WRONG, \"post-\" + attempt);\n        }\n        Harness.check(Harness.loginFrom(clear, EMAIL, Harness.PW_ONE, \"owner-final\").authenticated(),\n            \"a success cleared the counter - strikes do not accumulate across a successful sign-in\");\n        clear.close();\n        fixture.close();\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * The four refusal paths must cost the same amount of work, or response time tells an attacker\n     * whether an account exists. Both wall-clock and process CPU time are checked, and each path is\n     * floored absolutely: a ratio alone stays green when the work is deleted from every branch.\n     */\n    private static void equalWork(Harness.Fixture fixture) throws Exception {\n        Harness.section(\"every sign-in path performs one derivation, so timing cannot name the branch\");\n        Harness.registerStatus(fixture, EMAIL, NAME, Harness.PW_ONE, \"src-t\");\n\n        ThreadMXBean cpu = ManagementFactory.getThreadMXBean();\n        if (cpu.isThreadCpuTimeSupported() && !cpu.isThreadCpuTimeEnabled()) {\n            cpu.setThreadCpuTimeEnabled(true);\n        }\n        boolean cpuAccounting = cpu.isThreadCpuTimeSupported() && cpu.isThreadCpuTimeEnabled();\n        Harness.check(cpuAccounting,\n            \"thread CPU accounting is available, so key-derivation work cannot be hidden behind I/O\");\n\n        // JIT warm-up and first-touch page faults are not what is under test, so two throwaway\n        // lookups run before any sample is taken.\n        for (int warm = 0; warm < 2; warm++) {\n            Harness.loginFrom(fixture, \"warm\" + warm + \"@corp.example\", Harness.PW_WRONG, \"warm-\" + warm);\n        }\n\n        List<String> labels = List.of(\"malformed identifier\", \"unknown email\", \"unknown username\",\n            \"wrong password\", \"locked account\", \"correct password\");\n        List<Long> wall = new ArrayList<>();\n        List<Long> cpuMicros = new ArrayList<>();\n\n        Harness.equal(measure(fixture, cpu, \"!!malformed!!\", Harness.PW_WRONG, wall, cpuMicros),\n            AuthService.Status.INVALID_CREDENTIALS, \"the timed branch really was malformed input\");\n        Harness.equal(measure(fixture, cpu, \"nobody@corp.example\", Harness.PW_WRONG, wall, cpuMicros),\n            AuthService.Status.INVALID_CREDENTIALS, \"the timed branch really was an unknown mailbox\");\n        Harness.equal(measure(fixture, cpu, \"nobody-user\", Harness.PW_WRONG, wall, cpuMicros),\n            AuthService.Status.INVALID_CREDENTIALS, \"the timed branch really was an unknown username\");\n        Harness.equal(measure(fixture, cpu, NAME, Harness.PW_WRONG, wall, cpuMicros),\n            AuthService.Status.INVALID_CREDENTIALS, \"the timed branch really was a wrong password\");\n\n        // The samples above spent one strike each; burn the rest of the account budget so the next\n        // measurement really is the locked branch. Without this the \"locked\" timing would silently\n        // be a second wrong-password measurement and still pass.\n        for (int strike = SAMPLES; strike < Throttle.ACCOUNT_MAX_FAILURES; strike++) {\n            Harness.loginFrom(fixture, EMAIL, Harness.PW_WRONG, \"prime-\" + strike);\n        }\n        Harness.equal(measure(fixture, cpu, NAME, Harness.PW_WRONG, wall, cpuMicros),\n            AuthService.Status.THROTTLED, \"the timed branch really was a locked account\");\n\n        fixture.clock().advanceSeconds(Throttle.ACCOUNT_LOCK_SECONDS + 1);\n        Harness.equal(measure(fixture, cpu, NAME, Harness.PW_ONE, wall, cpuMicros),\n            AuthService.Status.AUTHENTICATED, \"the timed branch really was a successful sign-in\");\n\n        for (int i = 0; i < labels.size(); i++) {\n            Harness.atLeast(wall.get(i), DERIVATION_FLOOR_MICROS,\n                \"the \" + labels.get(i) + \" path is floored by one real derivation (wall)\");\n            if (cpuAccounting) {\n                Harness.atLeast(cpuMicros.get(i), DERIVATION_FLOOR_MICROS / 2,\n                    \"the \" + labels.get(i) + \" path burns CPU, not just elapsed time\");\n            }\n        }\n\n        double spread = (double) maxOf(wall) / (double) minOf(wall);\n        Harness.check(spread <= MAX_SPREAD_RATIO,\n            \"all six sign-in paths are within \" + MAX_SPREAD_RATIO + \"x of each other (saw \"\n                + String.format(\"%.2f\", spread) + \"x)\");\n        Harness.note(\"wall medians in microseconds: \" + pairUp(labels, wall));\n        if (cpuAccounting) {\n            Harness.note(\"cpu medians in microseconds: \" + pairUp(labels, cpuMicros));\n        }\n        Harness.note(\"slowest/fastest wall ratio across the six paths: \" + String.format(\"%.2f\", spread) + \"x\");\n        Harness.note(\"each derivation used m=\" + Harness.TEST_MEMORY_KIB + \"KiB,t=\"\n            + Harness.TEST_ITERATIONS + \",p=\" + Harness.TEST_PARALLELISM);\n        fixture.close();\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Takes {@link #SAMPLES} samples of one branch and records the medians. Every attempt uses a\n     * source key of its own, so the per-source budget can never be what answers a measurement.\n     *\n     * @return the status of the last sampled attempt, so the caller can prove which branch it timed\n     */\n    private static AuthService.Status measure(Harness.Fixture fixture, ThreadMXBean cpu,\n        String identifier, String password, List<Long> wall, List<Long> cpuMicros) {\n        List<Long> wallSamples = new ArrayList<>();\n        List<Long> cpuSamples = new ArrayList<>();\n        long key = TIMING_KEYS.incrementAndGet();\n        AuthService.Status last = null;\n        for (int sample = 0; sample < SAMPLES; sample++) {\n            long startedNanos = System.nanoTime();\n            long startedCpu = cpu.getCurrentThreadCpuTime();\n 1,\n                Harness.TEST_PARALLELISM);\n        } catch (java.security.GeneralSecurityException impossible) {\n            throw new IllegalStateException(impossible);\n        }\n        // fixture.clock());\n        AuthService raised = new AuthService(accounts, throttle, sessions, stronger, gate, fixture.clock());\n\n Harness.equal(after.kdfMemoryKib(), Harness.TEST_MEMORY_KIB * 2,\n            \"the new credential carries the raised memory cost\");\n        Harness.equal(after.kdfIterations(), Harness.TEST_ITERATIONS + 1,\n            \"the new credential carries the raised iteration count\");\n        Harness.check(stronger.verify(Harness.chars(Harness.PW_ONE), after.passwordHash()),\n rate-limit identity must be a kernel fact, not a launch-time property. A key\n     * derived from {@code System.getProperty(\"user.name\")} lets anyone who can add {@code\n     * -Duser.name=$RANDOM} mint a brand-new, full budget on every attempt, which makes the per-source\n     * limit decorative. This section mutates {@code user.name} between attempts on a fresh store: if\n     * the key tracked the property, no attempt would ever be throttled; because it tracks the uid, the\n     * shared budget is spent and the flood is refused.\n     */\n    private static void sourceIdentityIgnoresForgedName(Path dir) throws Exception {\n        Harness.section(\"a forged -Duser.name cannot mint a fresh rate-limit budget\");\n        Env env = env(dir, DB_PASSWORD);\n        String savedName = System.getProperty(\"user.name\");\n        try {\n            for (int attempt = 0; attempt < Throttle.SOURCE_MAX_ATTEMPTS + 3; attempt++) {\n                // A distinct user.name each time: the old property-derived key would treat every\n                // attempt as a different source and refill its budget.\n                System.setProperty(\"user.name\", \"attacker-\" + attempt);\n                run(dir, List.of(\"login\"),\n                    env.withInput(\"flood\" + attempt + \"@corp.example\\n\" + PASSWORD + \"\\n\"));\n            }\n        } finally {\n            if (savedName != null) {\n                System.setProperty(\"user.name\", savedName);\n            } else {\n                System.clearProperty(\"user.name\");\n            }\n        }\n        List<String> trail = Files.readAllLines(dir.resolve(\"audit.log\"), StandardCharsets.UTF_8);\n        Harness.requireNotEmpty(trail, \"the flood produced an audit trail to inspect\");\n        Harness.check(trail.stream().anyMatch(line -> line.contains(\"LOGIN_THROTTLED_SOURCE\")),\n            \"the attempts from differing user.name values shared one budget and ran into the per-source\"\n                + \" limit, so the key is not derived from a property a caller controls\");\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * {@code Path.of} raises an unchecked {@code InvalidPathException} for a path containing a NUL,\n     * which escapes configuration parsing and reaches the last-resort handler. That is the seam\n     * used here: the assertion is about what the handler prints, and a NUL makes the failure\n     * deterministic without adding a test-only hook to production code.\n     */\n    private static void crashIsReportedGenerically(Path dir) throws Exception {\n        Harness.section(\"an unchecked failure prints a generic refusal, not a stack trace\");\n        // One argument, the shipped form: as two arguments this is a malformed option and is refused\n        // by configuration, which is a different path and would leave the handler untested.\n        Run crashed = runRaw(List.of(\"login\", \"--data-dir=\" + dir + \"/hidden\\u0000dir\"),\n            dir, DB_PASSWORD, Harness.PW_ONE + \"\\n\");\n        Harness.equal((long) crashed.exit(), (long) \"the client sees the generic unavailability sentence, on the same channel as every other\"\n                + \" start-up failure\");\n        Harness.check(!crashed.out().contains(\"at app.\"),\n            \"nothing on stdout either\");\n        Harness.check(crashed.err().contains(\"NOTE unexpected failure of type\"),\n            \"the operator gets the exception's one-line identity to look up\");\n        Harness.check(!crashed.err().contains(\"at app.\") && !crashed.out().contains(\"at app.\")\n                && !crashed.err().contains(\"\\tat \"),\n            \"no stack trace is printed on either channel\");\n        Harness.check(!crashed.err().contains(\"hidden\") && !crashed.out().contains(\"hidden\"),\n            \"the refused path is not echoed - the exception's own message would name it\");\n        Harness.check(!crashed.err().contains(DB_PASSWORD) && !crashed.out().contains(DB_PASSWORD),\n            \"nor is the database password\");\n    }\n\n    // dir, String dbPassword, String input) {\n        Env withInput(String replacement) {\n            return new Env(dir, dbPassword, replacement);\n        }\n    }\n\n    private static Env env(Path dir, String dbPassword) {\n        return new Env(dir, dbPassword, \"\");\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Drives {@link Main#run} with an explicit environment function, so no test depends on the\n     * ambient shell environment or leaks state into another test.\n     */\n    private static Run run(Path dir, List<String> argv, Env environment) {\n        java.util.Map<String, String> values = new environment.dbPassword());\n        }\n        return invoke(argv, values, environment.input());\n    }\n\n    /** Same drive, but the caller owns argv entirely and the environment carries no data
